```cpp
Value *Val = SI->getValueOperand();

int64_t Offset = 0;
Value *RootPtr = stripPointerCastsAndOffsets(Ptr, /*Offset out*/ Offset);
// stripPointerCastsAndOffsets walks bitcast/addrspacecast/freeze/GEP and
// accumulates the constant GEP offset; returns nullptr if it encounters
// a non-constant GEP index, an inttoptr from a non-constant integer,
// or a ptrtoint round-trip.
auto BaseID = State.resolveVirtualRef(RootPtr);
```

**Case A — Ptr resolves to a virtual object, Offset is constant**:

1. Find or create the field index:
   `int FI = VObj->getOrCreateFieldIndex(Offset, ExpectedTypeOfField(Val));`
   `ExpectedTypeOfField` is `Val->getType()` adjusted for the in-memory
   representation (boolean stores are emitted as `i8 & 1`; the stored
   type is `i8` even though the Java view is `Z`).
2. **Width/overlap check** (consult Graal's
   `VirtualizerToolImpl.setVirtualEntry` for the canonical table):
   - Same access kind / same size → straightforward update.
   - Long store covering two consecutive int slots (`offset % 8 == 0`
     AND next i32 slot exists) → split the long into two ints via the
     `UnpackEndianHalfNode` equivalent (synthesize `trunc` and
     `lshr`+`trunc`).
   - Byte-array reinterpretation (writing an i32 into a virtual byte
     array): only permitted if `canVirtualizeLargeByteArrayUnsafeAccess()`
     (set to false initially — force materialization on byte array
     writes wider than a byte).
   - Mismatched non-overlapping kinds at the same offset in an instance
     field (writing a float over a tracked int): materialise.

   When the check fails: `materialise(BaseID)` to spill, then leave the
   store in place (Tier-2 generic case 2.3.18 takes over).

3. **Value tag**:
   - If `Val` resolves through the alias map to a virtual ObjectID
     `RefID`: `Entries[FI] = FieldValue::virtualRef(RefID)`.
   - Else if `Val` is a `Constant`:
     `Entries[FI] = FieldValue::scalar(Val)`.
   - Else: `Entries[FI] = FieldValue::scalar(Val)`.
4. Apply COW on the ObjectState before mutating:
   `State.getObjectStateForModification(BaseID).Entries[FI] = ...;`.
5. Record `Effect::EliminateStore(Target=SI, Block=currentBlock)`.
6. **Atomicity**: irrelevant for the eliminated store — the scalar
   replacement is thread-local. `EliminateStore` removes SI entirely
   regardless of ordering.
7. **Volatile + sync scope**: irrelevant once eliminated.
8. **Metadata** (`!nontemporal`, `!align`, `!noundef`): discarded with
   the store; no propagation needed because no memory op remains.
9. SI has no result (`void`); no users to mark.

**Case B — Ptr resolves to a virtual object but Offset is non-constant**:
the store could land at any byte within the object. We cannot track
precisely. `materialise(BaseID)` BEFORE SI; leave SI in place. The store
then writes to the materialised heap object correctly.

**Case C — Ptr does not resolve to a virtual object, Val resolves to a
virtual ObjectID**: writing a virtual reference into non-virtual memory
publishes it; the virtual object escapes.
- `materialise(RefID)` BEFORE SI.
- `Effect::ReplaceInput(Target=SI, InputIndex=0,
  Replacement=materializedPtr)`.
- SI is preserved.

**Case D — Neither Ptr nor Val is virtual**: no PEA action. SI is not in
`HasVirtualInputs`.

**`atomicrmw` variant**.
```cpp
auto *AR = dyn_cast<AtomicRMWInst>(I);
// AR->getPointerOperand(), AR->getValOperand(), AR->getOperation()
// (Xchg, Add, Sub, And, Nand, Or, Xor, Max, Min, UMax, UMin, FAdd,
//  FSub, FMax, FMin, UIncWrap, UDecWrap)
```
If `AR->getPointerOperand()` resolves to a virtual object: RMW semantics
require observable atomic memory access. We cannot faithfully emulate
them on scalar-replaced fields without weakening ordering guarantees.
**Action**: always materialise the virtual base, leave AR in place. The
result of AR (the old value) is unchanged.

Future optimisation (out of scope for v1): if the RMW is `Xchg` on a
field with no other reader in scope AND ordering is `Monotonic`, we
could rewrite to a plain store of the new value and replace AR's result
with the prior field value.

**`cmpxchg` variant**.
```cpp
auto *CX = dyn_cast<AtomicCmpXchgInst>(I);
// CX->getPointerOperand(), CX->getCompareOperand(), CX->getNewValOperand()
// Result type { ExpectedTy, i1 }: extractvalue 0 = loaded old value,
// extractvalue 1 = success flag.
```
Following Graal's `AbstractUnsafeCompareAndSwapNode.virtualize`:

1. Resolve pointer to (BaseID, Offset) as in Case A. Require constant
   Offset; else materialise and leave CX in place.
2. Look up current `Entries[FI]` (synthesize a load result; see 2.3.4
   for type-mismatch handling).
3. Resolve `compare` and `newValue` via the alias map.
4. If both `compare` and current entry are scalar AND their equality can
   be folded statically:
   - Equal: cmpxchg succeeds. Update `Entries[FI] = newValue` (with
     alias/virtualRef tagging from Case A). Replace CX with constant
     `{ oldValue, i1 true }`. Record `Effect::EliminateStore(CX)` and
     `Effect::ReplaceCall(CX, ConstantStruct{oldValue, true})`.
   - Statically unequal: cmpxchg fails; object unchanged. Replace CX
     with `{ oldValue, i1 false }`.
5. If comparison cannot be folded statically OR either operand is a
   virtual ref whose identity comparison is symbolic: synthesize an
   `icmp eq` for the runtime comparison and a `select` for the new
   field value (matches Graal's `ConditionalNode`). Record:
   - `Effect::AddNode` for the new `icmp` and `select` (inserted before CX),
   - update `Entries[FI] = FieldValue::scalar(selectedValue)`,
   - `Effect::EliminateStore(CX)`,
   - `Effect::ReplaceCall(CX, ConstantStruct::get(...))` where the i1
     becomes the icmp result and the value becomes the current entry's
     prior value.
6. If `newValue` is a virtual ref AND we cannot store it (width
   mismatch): materialise `newValue` first; re-run the rule.

**`weak` cmpxchg**: identical handling; the weak flag has no effect on a
fully simulated scalar replacement.

**`memcpy / memmove / memset` to a virtual object**.
```cpp
auto *II = dyn_cast<MemIntrinsic>(I);
// MemCpyInst, MemMoveInst, MemSetInst
```
- `memset(virtualPtr, value, len)`: if `len` is constant,
  `len <= VObj->SizeInBytes`, and the start offset (relative to the
  virtual object's base) is constant: iterate fields whose byte ranges
  fall within `[startOffset, startOffset+len)` and set each to the
  splat constant of `value` (per field type). If `value` is
  non-constant, only the splatted bit pattern of a single byte for byte
  fields is valid; for wider fields, materialise.
- `memcpy(virtualDst, src, len)` / `memmove(virtualDst, src, len)`:
  - if `src` is a virtual object pointer with the same layout and
    matching ranges: copy each field entry src → dst.
  - if `src` is a non-virtual constant array (e.g. a `@.str` global):
    decompose the constant into per-field values.
  - otherwise: materialise virtualDst (and virtualSrc if also virtual)
    and leave the intrinsic in place.

For v1: simplest conservative implementation — if either operand of
memcpy/memmove/memset resolves to a virtual object, materialise that
object. Per-field handling above is a future optimisation.

**Aggregate `insertvalue`/`extractvalue`** operate on first-class
aggregates, not memory; they cannot mutate a virtual object's fields.
Handled in 2.3.18 (generic) — never causes escape because their operands
cannot be virtual `ptr addrspace(1)`. Defensively, if an `insertvalue`
produces a value containing a virtual ref and that value is later stored
to memory, the store handler in Case C materialises correctly.

**Stores via `inttoptr` from a non-constant integer**: can launder a
virtual ref via `ptrtoint`. Handling:
- `stripPointerCastsAndOffsets` refuses to traverse `inttoptr` (returns
  nullptr). Case A's BaseID lookup fails. Falls into Case C or D
  depending on Val.
- If Val resolves to a virtual ref through any chain, materialise
  (Case C).
- If Val is also `inttoptr`-laundered, the alias chain through
  `inttoptr` must NOT be traversed (we treat `inttoptr` as opaque, so
  Val is not a virtual ref from PEA's perspective). The round-trip
  `ptrtoint(virtualRef) -> ... -> inttoptr -> store` is detected at the
  `ptrtoint` site — see 2.3.16 / 2.3.16a.

**GC barrier interaction**. PEA runs BEFORE InsertGCBarriers in the
canonical pipeline; eliminated stores are not yet accompanied by barrier
calls. If PEA is moved AFTER InsertGCBarriers, the transform pass must
also remove `@jeandle.g1_pre_barrier`, `@jeandle.g1_post_barrier`, and
`@jeandle.g1_pre_barrier_loaded` calls targeting the eliminated store's
address. Barrier calls are recognisable by name and by their argument
referring to the same address computation as the store.

#### 2.3.4 Load: `load [atomic|volatile] %ty, ptr addrspace(N) %ptr`

**Detection**: `dyn_cast<LoadInst>(I)`.

**Resolve target** as in 2.3.3:
```cpp
Value *Ptr = LI->getPointerOperand();
int64_t Offset = 0;
Value *RootPtr = stripPointerCastsAndOffsets(Ptr, Offset);
auto BaseID = State.resolveVirtualRef(RootPtr);
```

**Case A — Ptr resolves to a virtual object, Offset constant**:

1. Look up `FI = VObj->getFieldIndex(Offset)`. If FI == -1 (no store has
   touched this offset and it's not the array length slot), the field
   value is its zero/null initial value:
   - Primitive types: `Constant` of the appropriate type (i32 0,
     double 0.0, etc.).
   - Reference types (`ptr addrspace(1)`): `ConstantPointerNull`.
   - Synthesise the constant; record
     `Effect::ReplaceLoad(Target=LI, Replacement=zeroConst)`.
   - `AliasMap::addScalarAlias(LI, zeroConst)`.

2. If FI is valid, inspect `Entries[FI]`:
   - `Unknown` (no store recorded): same as FI == -1 — replace with
     zero.
   - `Scalar`: take the stored Value `V`. Check load type vs `V->getType()`:
     - Identical types: replacement = V.
     - Same size, different scalar kind (stored `i32`, loaded `float`):
       synthesise `bitcast` (record `Effect::AddNode`); replacement =
       the bitcast.
     - Different sizes (stored `i64`, loaded `i32` at same offset):
       per Graal's `RawLoadNode.virtualize`, only certain
       transformations are legal:
       - load is `i8/i16/i32`, stored `i64`: synthesise `trunc V to
         loadType` for the low half (`Effect::AddNode`).
       - load offset within field is non-zero (e.g. loading the high
         half): synthesise `lshr` followed by `trunc`.
       - if not expressible (e.g. partial reads on a reference field):
         materialise the object, leave LI in place.
     - Reference-to-primitive cast (loading `i64` from stored
       `ptr addrspace(1)` field via Unsafe): only allowed when
       `canVirtualizeLargeByteArrayUnsafeAccess()`; otherwise
       materialise.
     - Primitive-to-reference cast (loading `ptr addrspace(1)` from a
       stored `i64`): always materialise — fabricating a reference value
       from a tracked integer is unsound for GC.

   - `VirtualRef(RefID)`: the loaded value is itself a virtual ref.
     - `AliasMap::addVirtualAlias(LI, RefID, VirtualObjects[RefID].get())`.
     - Record `Effect::AddVirtualAlias(Target=LI, ObjID=RefID, ...)`.
     - No `ReplaceLoad` effect — the load's "value" is purely abstract
       until the transform pass materialises if needed.

   - `MaterializedRef(Ptr)`: the field already holds a pointer to a
     materialised heap object. Replacement = Ptr (bitcast if types
     differ).
     - Record `Effect::ReplaceLoad(Target=LI, Replacement=Ptr)`.
     - `AliasMap::addScalarAlias(LI, Ptr)`.

3. **Metadata propagation** when replacing LI:
   - `!nonnull` on LI: propagate to the replacement if the replacement
     is a reference and known non-null (virtual refs are never null;
     virtual objects are never null).
   - `!range` on LI: drop unless the replacement is a constant within
     range (then no-op).
   - `!java-klass` / `!java-klass-exact` on LI: propagate to the
     replacement if (i) the replacement is a load we are inserting via
     `Effect::AddNode`, or (ii) the replacement's type matches and the
     field's static klass is known. Drop if the replacement is null.
   - `!invariant.load`: drop (replacement IS the stored value).
   - `!noundef`: propagate if the replacement is defined.
   - `!align`, `!dereferenceable`: drop (no memory op remains).
   - `!tbaa`: drop.

4. **Atomicity / volatility**: as with stores, the eliminated load
   carries no memory operation; atomicity is moot. The replacement
   value is read from a tracked local — semantically equivalent to a
   thread-local read, no fencing needed.

5. **Sync scope**: ignored for elimination.

**Case B — Ptr resolves to a virtual object but Offset non-constant**:
materialise; leave LI in place.

**Case C — Ptr does not resolve to a virtual object**: no PEA action.
LI is not in `HasVirtualInputs`.

**Special "info" loads**. The Jeandle frontend currently uses dedicated
calls (`jeandle.arraylength`, `jeandle.load_klass`) for klass and array
length, not direct `LoadInst`s. If PEA runs at a position where those
calls have been lowered to `LoadInst`s plus offsets, the rules in 2.3.7
and 2.3.8 also apply via the generic load path here. Specifically:
- Load at offset `arrayOopDesc::length_offset_in_bytes` on a virtual
  array: return the stored `ArrayLengthVal` constant.
- Load at offset `oopDesc::klass_offset_in_bytes` (compressed or not):
  return the klass constant as `inttoptr i64 K to ptr` (addrspace 0).
These special offsets are recorded in `VirtualObject` at construction
time and handled by `getFieldIndex` returning the length/klass slots.

#### 2.3.5 Monitor enter: `call i1 @jeandle.monitorenter_with_*(obj, lock)`

**Detection**.
```cpp
StringRef N = CB->getCalledFunction()->getName();
bool isMonEnter =
       N == "jeandle.monitorenter_with_monitor_lock"     // LM_MONITOR
    || N == "jeandle.monitorenter_with_thin_lock"        // LM_LEGACY
    || N == "jeandle.monitorenter_with_lightweight_lock";// LM_LIGHTWEIGHT
```
These three variants are emitted by `JeandleAbstractInterpreter` based
on the runtime's `LockingMode`. All have signature
`i1 (ptr addrspace(1), ptr addrspace(0))`. **Lock object is argument 0**
(the Java oop, addrspace 1). **Lock record is argument 1** (a
stack-allocated lock record, addrspace 0). Confusing these is a common
bug source — argument 0 is what we virtualise.

**Pre-conditions**.
1. `obj = CB->getArgOperand(0)` resolves to a virtual ObjectID.
2. `LockingMode` permits virtualisation:
   - LM_MONITOR (variant 0): always permitted; locks have side effects
     but the runtime accepts unstructured monitorenter/monitorexit
     orderings (see Graal's `MonitorEnterNode.virtualize` comment).
   - LM_LEGACY (variant 1 / thin lock): same as above.
   - LM_LIGHTWEIGHT (variant 2): permitted ONLY if no virtual object
     with a lower lock depth would become materialised on any successor
     edge. Mirrors Graal's `requiresStrictLockOrder` check: before
     adding the lock, scan all `VirtualObjects` and for each whose
     `ObjectState.isVirtual()` AND `LockCount > 0` AND
     `MinimumLockDepth < currentLockDepth`, materialise that other
     object FIRST. Failure to do so would create unstructured locking
     on the lightweight-lock stack, observable by stack scanners.

3. Platform supports virtual locks. Mirroring Graal's
   `getPlatformConfigurationProvider().areLocksSideEffectFree()`: if the
   runtime configuration says locks are side-effect-free, we may
   eliminate without further ado; otherwise we may still eliminate but
   would need to `ensureAdded` a `StateSplitProxy` (Jeandle has no
   FrameState, so this is a no-op for us).

4. CB is not in a `funclet` operand-bundle scope (Windows EH). If
   present, treat as generic escape (2.3.18).

**Actions on success**.
- COW: `State.getObjectStateForModification(ID).addLock();` (increments
  `LockCount` and records the depth).
- Record `Effect::EliminateLock(Target=CB, Block=currentBlock)`.
- Virtual locks always "succeed": record
  `Effect::ReplaceCall(Target=CB,
  Replacement=ConstantInt::getTrue(I1))`.
- `AliasMap::addScalarAlias(CB, ConstantInt::getTrue(...))` so any later
  `br i1 CB` collapses via Tier 3.

**Re-entrant locking**. Multiple monitorenters on the same virtual
object increment `LockCount` repeatedly; each matched monitorexit
decrements. This is Graal's `state.addLock` / `state.removeLock` with
monitor identity equality. Jeandle does NOT track distinct MonitorId
identities; two monitorenters on the same VirtualObject are assumed
re-entrant.

**`InvokeInst` vs `CallInst`**. Both monitorenter and monitorexit can be
either. Jeandle's fast-path variants do not throw (they bail to the
runtime through a separate slow path outside the JavaOp wrapper), so
they are typically emitted as `CallInst`. Handle both.

**Operand bundles**:
- `"deopt"`: not currently emitted on these fast-path calls. If
  present, ignored.
- `"funclet"`: Windows EH; refuse virtualisation.
- `"gc-live"`, `"gc-result"`: post-RS4GC; not at PEA's position.

#### 2.3.6 Monitor exit: `call i1 @jeandle.monitorexit_with_*(obj, lock)`

**Detection**: analogous to 2.3.5 with `monitorexit_with_monitor_lock`,
`monitorexit_with_thin_lock`, `monitorexit_with_lightweight_lock`.

**Pre-conditions**.
1. `obj = CB->getArgOperand(0)` resolves to a virtual ObjectID with
   `LockCount > 0`.
2. Mirrors Graal's `MonitorExitNode.virtualize`: requires
   `virtual.hasIdentity()` (always true in Jeandle — see Section 1.1
   note on `IsExact`).

**Actions**.
- COW: `State.getObjectStateForModification(ID).removeLock();`.
- If `LockCount` would go below zero (unbalanced unlock — invalid Java
  but possible in malformed IR): materialise the object and leave the
  call in place to let the runtime detect the violation. Do NOT crash.
- Record `Effect::EliminateLock(Target=CB)`.
- Record `Effect::ReplaceCall(Target=CB,
  Replacement=ConstantInt::getTrue(I1))`.
- `AliasMap::addScalarAlias(CB, true)`.

**LM_LIGHTWEIGHT special case**: if we materialised some other virtual
object earlier in this block due to `requiresStrictLockOrder` (2.3.5),
the matching monitorexits for those materialised objects must occur
BEFORE this monitorexit in the same control region. The
`materialiseVirtualLocksBefore` logic from Graal's PartialEscapeClosure
does this by re-issuing lock effects on the materialised path; we
replicate it here. Specifically:
- When materialising at 2.3.5, also record `Effect::Materialize` with
  the full `LockCount` so the transform pass emits N matching synthetic
  monitorenter calls.
- When the matching monitorexit is observed (2.3.6), if the object is
  already materialised, leave the call in place (it operates on the
  real heap object).

#### 2.3.7 `call i32 @jeandle.arraylength(obj)` on virtual array

**Detection**:
`CB->getCalledFunction()->getName() == "jeandle.arraylength"`.

**Pre-conditions**: `obj = CB->getArgOperand(0)` resolves to a virtual
ObjectID with `Kind == Array`.

**Actions**.
- Replacement is `VObj->ArrayLengthVal` (the `ConstantInt` captured at
  allocation time).
- Record `Effect::ReplaceCall(Target=CB,
  Replacement=ArrayLengthVal)`.
- `AliasMap::addScalarAlias(CB, ArrayLengthVal)`.

**Metadata**. The call's return is implicitly `[0, MaxArrayLength]` and
non-negative. The replacement constant trivially satisfies this. No
metadata propagation needed.

**`CallInst` vs `InvokeInst`**: `jeandle.arraylength` is `noinline` but
does not throw (only the front-end null check before it can throw);
typically emitted as `CallInst`. Handle both.

**Non-virtual case**: if `obj` does not resolve to a virtual ObjectID,
no PEA action; CB is not in `HasVirtualInputs`.

#### 2.3.8 `call ptr addrspace(0) @jeandle.load_klass(obj)` on virtual object

**Detection**:
`CB->getCalledFunction()->getName() == "jeandle.load_klass"`.

**Return type**: `ptr addrspace(0)` (the klass lives in metaspace, NOT
the Java heap). The replacement value MUST be addrspace 0, not
addrspace 1.

**Pre-conditions**: `obj = CB->getArgOperand(0)` resolves to a virtual
ObjectID. Either Kind.

**Actions**.
- For instance objects, the klass is `VObj->Klass`.
- For array objects, the klass is `VObj->ArrayKlass`.
- Synthesise the constant:
  ```cpp
  Value *KlassConst = ConstantExpr::getIntToPtr(
      ConstantInt::get(Int64Ty, KlassPtr),
      PointerType::get(Ctx, /*addrspace*/0));
  ```
- Record `Effect::ReplaceCall(Target=CB, Replacement=KlassConst)`.
- `AliasMap::addScalarAlias(CB, KlassConst)`.

**Metadata**. The replacement is `nonnull` (klass pointers are never
null for materialised objects). If CB carried `!nonnull` or
`!dereferenceable`, the constant trivially satisfies them.

**Compressed klass**: if `UseCompressedClassPointers` is on, the runtime
representation differs but the IR-level type is still `ptr addrspace(0)`.
Compression / decompression happens at lowering time. PEA does not need
to handle compression here.

#### 2.3.9 `call i1 @jeandle.checkcast(super_klass, obj)` on virtual object

**Detection**:
`CB->getCalledFunction()->getName() == "jeandle.checkcast"`. Argument
order: `arg0 = super_klass (ptr addrspace(0))`,
`arg1 = obj (ptr addrspace(1))`. Returns i1 (true if cast succeeds).

**Pre-conditions**:
- `arg1` resolves to a virtual ObjectID.
- `arg0` reduces via `extractKlassConstant(arg0)` to a non-null klass
  constant `SuperK`.

**Actions** (mirror of Graal's check via `IsSubtype`):

1. `bool IsSub = VMCallbacks::IsSubtype(VObj->Klass, SuperK);`
   - `VObj->Klass` is always exact (java-klass-exact). So `IsSub==true`
     ⟹ statically passes; `IsSub==false` AND klasses incompatible
     (`areKlassesIncompatible(VObj->Klass, /*exact*/true, SuperK)`) ⟹
     statically fails.
2. If statically passes:
   - Replacement = `ConstantInt::getTrue(I1)`.
   - `Effect::ReplaceCall(Target=CB, Replacement=true)`.
   - `AliasMap::addScalarAlias(CB, true)`.
3. If statically fails:
   - Replacement = `ConstantInt::getFalse(I1)`.
   - `Effect::ReplaceCall(Target=CB, Replacement=false)`.
   - `AliasMap::addScalarAlias(CB, false)`.
   - If CB's next instruction is `br i1 CB, ifTrue, ifFalse`: record
     `Effect::KillBranch(Target=br, Block=currentBlock,
     BranchCondition=false)` to fold the branch directly to `ifFalse`.
4. If neither (e.g. SuperK is an interface and the answer is
   indeterminate without runtime check): leave CB in place. Rare —
   exact-type instance allocations usually answer definitively.

**Interface super types**: `IsSubtype` handles
`super_klass.isInterface()` via VMCallbacks, which knows the interface
hierarchy.

**`checkcast` of null**: defined to succeed (return null). Since virtual
objects are never null, this case does not arise here — only relevant
for non-virtual operands.

#### 2.3.10 `call i1 @jeandle.check_instanceof(super_klass, obj)` / `call i32 @jeandle.instanceof(super_klass, obj)` on virtual object

**Detection**: callee name `jeandle.check_instanceof` (returns i1; obj
arg has `nonnull`) or `jeandle.instanceof` (returns i32; obj may be
null).

**Pre-conditions**: same as 2.3.9 plus, for `instanceof`, the result is
`1` if obj is non-null AND a subtype, `0` otherwise. Since virtual
objects are never null, the null case is eliminated:

For `check_instanceof`:
- Statically a subtype → `ConstantInt::getTrue(I1)`.
- Statically not a subtype → `ConstantInt::getFalse(I1)`.

For `instanceof`:
- Statically a subtype → `ConstantInt::get(I32, 1)`.
- Statically not a subtype → `ConstantInt::get(I32, 0)`.

Record `Effect::ReplaceCall` accordingly. `KillBranch` applies when the
call's only user is a `br i1` (for `check_instanceof`) or an
`icmp ne %res, 0` followed by `br` (for `instanceof`).

**Precondition `nonnull` on `check_instanceof`**: the caller has
null-checked obj. Virtual objects trivially satisfy this (just
allocated), so it is safe to propagate the result without re-checking.

#### 2.3.11 Null check: `icmp eq/ne ptr addrspace(1) %obj, null`

**Detection**:
```cpp
auto *IC = dyn_cast<ICmpInst>(I);
if (!IC || (IC->getPredicate() != ICmpInst::ICMP_EQ &&
            IC->getPredicate() != ICmpInst::ICMP_NE))
  return false;
Value *Lhs = IC->getOperand(0), *Rhs = IC->getOperand(1);
bool LhsNull = isa<ConstantPointerNull>(Lhs);
bool RhsNull = isa<ConstantPointerNull>(Rhs);
if (!LhsNull && !RhsNull) return false;
Value *NonNullOp = LhsNull ? Rhs : Lhs;
```

**Pre-conditions**: `NonNullOp` resolves to a virtual ObjectID.

**Actions**: virtual objects are never null. The icmp result is:
- `EQ`: `false` (`ConstantInt::getFalse(I1)`).
- `NE`: `true`.

Record `Effect::ReplaceCall(Target=IC, Replacement=...)`.
`AliasMap::addScalarAlias(IC, replacement)` — any `br i1 IC` collapses
via Tier 3.

**Vector icmp**: `icmp eq <N x ptr addrspace(1)>, zeroinitializer` is
legal but not currently emitted by Jeandle. If encountered, fall through
to 2.3.18 (generic) and materialise.

**`!nonnull` metadata on `NonNullOp`** (e.g. on a function parameter):
already handled by the entry block's `NonNullArgs` table at Tier 3. The
Tier-2 path here only fires when `NonNullOp` is virtual.

#### 2.3.12 Equality comparison: `icmp eq/ne ptr addrspace(1) %a, %b`

**Detection**: as 2.3.11 but both operands non-null (null case handled
there).

**Resolve both operands**:
```cpp
ObjectID IDx = State.resolveVirtualRef(IC->getOperand(0));
ObjectID IDy = State.resolveVirtualRef(IC->getOperand(1));
```

**Cases** (mirror of Graal's `ObjectEqualsNode.virtualizeComparison`):

1. Both resolve to the SAME ObjectID: `==` is true, `!=` is false.
   - All Jeandle virtual objects have identity (no `VirtualBoxingNode`
     analogue), so this is unambiguous.
   - Record `Effect::ReplaceCall(IC, true/false)`.

2. Both resolve to DIFFERENT ObjectIDs: `==` is false, `!=` is true.
   Two distinct allocations have distinct identity.
   - `Effect::ReplaceCall(IC, false/true)`.

3. One operand is virtual, the other is not virtual:
   - A virtual object cannot equal any pre-existing heap reference, any
     materialised pointer of a DIFFERENT virtual object, or null.
   - `==` is false, `!=` is true.
   - Subtle case: if the non-virtual operand is the MATERIALISED pointer
     of the SAME virtual ObjectID (state has transitioned
     Virtual→Materialized between the two operands' definitions), they
     ARE equal. `resolveVirtualRef` chases through
     `ObjectState.MaterializedPtr` and returns the original ObjectID.

4. Neither resolves to a virtual ObjectID: no PEA action.

**`ptrtoint` round-trip**: if one operand is
`ptrtoint(virtualRef) -> ... -> inttoptr -> %x`, we lose virtual identity
through the cast. v1 policy: force materialisation at
`ptrtoint(virtualRef)` (2.3.18 generic — `ptrtoint` is not a recognised
virtualizable pattern, so its virtual operand is materialised).

**Three-way (`<=>`) style sequences**: any `select` or `phi` whose
condition is the result of an equality comparison handled here will be
folded by Tier 3 once the icmp is replaced by a constant.

#### 2.3.13 `call i1 @jeandle.check_if_value_based(obj)` on virtual object

**Detection**: callee name `jeandle.check_if_value_based`. Signature
`i1 (ptr addrspace(1))`.

**Pre-conditions**: `obj` resolves to a virtual ObjectID.

**Actions**:

1. `bool IsVB = VMCallbacks::IsValueBased(VObj->Klass);` (true for
   `@jdk.internal.ValueBased`-annotated classes such as `Integer`,
   `Optional`, etc.).
2. If IsVB is true:
   - Value-based classes have non-identity-comparable semantics; a
     monitor on one is illegal.
   - v1 policy: materialise (conservative; matches Graal). The
     `check_if_value_based` call remains to perform the runtime
     check on the materialised object.
3. If IsVB is false:
   - Replacement = `ConstantInt::getFalse(I1)`.
   - `Effect::ReplaceCall(IC, false)`.
   - `AliasMap::addScalarAlias(IC, false)`.

#### 2.3.14 Other calls with virtual object arguments

**Detection**: any `CallBase` whose callee is NOT one of: `jeandle.new_instance`,
`jeandle.newarray` (Tier 1), `jeandle.arraylength`, `jeandle.load_klass`,
`jeandle.checkcast`, `jeandle.check_instanceof`, `jeandle.instanceof`,
the monitor enter/exit variants, `jeandle.check_if_value_based`,
`jeandle.array_store_check`, or one of the enumerated non-escaping LLVM
intrinsics below.

**Action**: for each argument that resolves to a virtual ObjectID,
materialise that object BEFORE the call. Record
`Effect::ReplaceInput(Target=CB, InputIndex=argIdx,
Replacement=materializedPtr)`. Repeat for every virtual argument. The
call itself is preserved (escape point: e.g. a generic Java method
invocation).

**Recognised non-escaping JavaOps** (do NOT cause escape, fully handled
in their dedicated rules):
- `jeandle.arraylength` — 2.3.7
- `jeandle.load_klass` — 2.3.8
- `jeandle.checkcast` — 2.3.9
- `jeandle.check_instanceof` — 2.3.10
- `jeandle.instanceof` — 2.3.10
- `jeandle.monitorenter_with_monitor_lock` — 2.3.5
- `jeandle.monitorenter_with_thin_lock` — 2.3.5
- `jeandle.monitorenter_with_lightweight_lock` — 2.3.5
- `jeandle.monitorexit_with_monitor_lock` — 2.3.6
- `jeandle.monitorexit_with_thin_lock` — 2.3.6
- `jeandle.monitorexit_with_lightweight_lock` — 2.3.6
- `jeandle.check_if_value_based` — 2.3.13
- `jeandle.array_store_check` — see below.

**`jeandle.array_store_check(value, array_oop)`**: checks
element-vs-array-type compatibility. It does NOT publish the value (it
only inspects the klasses). Treat as non-escaping:
- If `array_oop` resolves to a virtual array AND the element klass is
  statically known to be a supertype of `value`'s klass: replace with
  `ConstantInt::getTrue(I1)`. Record `Effect::ReplaceCall(CB, true)`.
- If statically incompatible: replace with false (and `KillBranch` if
  appropriate).
- If neither operand is virtual or the types cannot be resolved: leave
  the call in place. It is read-only with respect to the heap; no
  virtual object escapes through it.

**Recognised non-escaping LLVM intrinsics** (do NOT cause escape):
- `llvm.assume(i1)`: pure assume. If the i1 involves a virtual ref
  (e.g. `assume(icmp ne ptr_virtual, null)`), Tier 3 should already
  have folded it to true; assume(true) is a no-op. Action: leave;
  never materialise on its account.
- `llvm.lifetime.start(i64, ptr)` and `llvm.lifetime.end(i64, ptr)` on
  a ptr derived from a virtual ref: no-op markers. Do NOT materialise.
  The transform pass removes them when their pointer's allocation is
  removed.
- `llvm.invariant.start(i64, ptr)` and `llvm.invariant.end(...)`: same
  as lifetime — markers; never materialise.
- `llvm.experimental.noalias.scope.decl`: pure metadata; no escape.
- `llvm.dbg.*` (declare, value, addr, label, assign): debug intrinsics;
  markers. Never materialise. Transform pass removes them alongside the
  allocation.
- `llvm.experimental.gc.statepoint`, `llvm.experimental.gc.result`,
  `llvm.experimental.gc.relocate`: added by RewriteStatepointsForGC,
  which runs AFTER PEA. Will not appear at PEA's position. If PEA is
  ever moved AFTER RS4GC (out of scope for v1), virtual references in
  `gc.live` operand bundles must trigger materialisation.
- `llvm.gcroot(ptr, ptr)` and other shadow-stack GC intrinsics: Jeandle
  uses statepoint + addrspace(1), NOT shadow stack. Do not expect to
  see `llvm.gcroot` in Jeandle IR. If seen, generic escape (2.3.18).
- `llvm.memcpy.*`, `llvm.memmove.*`, `llvm.memset.*`: handled in 2.3.3
  (mem-intrinsic variant).
- `llvm.expect`, `llvm.expect.with.probability`: branch hints. i1/i32
  operands; cannot carry a virtual ref. No special handling.
- `llvm.ctlz`, `llvm.cttz`, `llvm.ctpop`, `llvm.bswap`,
  `llvm.bitreverse`, `llvm.fshl`, `llvm.fshr`, `llvm.fma`, `llvm.sqrt`,
  saturating arithmetic: operate on primitives only.
- `llvm.is.constant`: any type. If the argument is a virtual ref,
  replace with `ConstantInt::getFalse(I1)` (the virtual ref is not a
  Constant at the LLVM level even if its identity is statically
  known). Avoids materialisation. Record `Effect::ReplaceCall`.
- `llvm.donothing`, `llvm.sideeffect`: pure markers; no escape.
- `llvm.coro.*`: not used in Jeandle; if seen, generic escape.

**Operand bundles on calls**:
- `"deopt"(...)`: arguments inside this bundle are NOT considered
  escapes by PEA. The bundle is debug-state for deoptimisation; Jeandle
  has no deopt infrastructure (out of scope). The transform pass
  preserves the bundle verbatim on any call it materialises, but PEA
  does not scan the bundle's operands for virtual refs.
- `"funclet"(token)`: Windows EH; refuse to optimise around (generic
  escape).
- `"gc-live"(...)`, `"gc-result"(...)`: RS4GC bundles; not at PEA's
  position. If seen, treat operands as escapes.
- `"ptrauth"(...)`: not used in Jeandle. Opaque.
- `"clang.arc.attachedcall"(...)`: not used. Opaque.

**Inline assembly**: `CB->isInlineAsm()`. Always generic escape
(2.3.18) — the asm string can do anything; any virtual ref reaching
inline asm must be materialised.

**`musttail`, `notail`, `tail` markers**: do not affect virtualisation
decisions; the call still escapes any virtual ref it receives.
`musttail` does prevent inserting materialisation code AFTER the call
(which we wouldn't do — materialisations are inserted BEFORE the escape
point).

#### 2.3.15 Return: `ret`, `resume`, `unreachable`

**`ret %ty %val`** (RetInst):
- If `%val` resolves to a virtual ObjectID: materialise BEFORE the
  return. Record `Effect::Materialize` and
  `Effect::ReplaceInput(Target=ret, InputIndex=0,
  Replacement=materializedPtr)`. Materialisation point is the ret's
  immediate predecessor instruction.
- If `%val` is not virtual: no action.
- Multiple returns: each `ret` processed independently in its block.
  No need to merge to a single exit (SimplifyCFG may later merge them).
- `ret void`: no operand, no action.
- Return type `ptr addrspace(1)` and `%val` null or non-virtual: no
  PEA action.

**`resume { ptr, i32 } %ehVal`** (ResumeInst): an exception is being
re-thrown. The exception value (a `ptr addrspace(1)` inside the
`{ ptr, i32 }` aggregate) carries a Java reference.
- If the exception value extracted (via `extractvalue`) from the resume
  operand resolves to a virtual ObjectID: materialise BEFORE the
  resume. The thrown reference escapes to the caller's catch block.
- Record `Effect::Materialize` and the appropriate `ReplaceInput`. In
  practice the resume operand is built via `insertvalue` from the
  landingpad result, so the materialisation target is the `insertvalue`
  chain.

**`unreachable`** (UnreachableInst): no operands, no action. Often
follows a `noreturn` call (which would have escaped any virtual ref
already via 2.3.14) or a folded dead branch. No-op for PEA.

**`indirectbr`, `switch`, `br`**: no value-typed operands carrying
references; no action. (`switch` cases are constant ints, `br`'s
condition is i1, `indirectbr` targets are block addresses.)

**`callbr`** (asm goto): treat the indirect-call portion as inline asm
(generic escape for any virtual ref argument).

**`catchret`, `cleanupret`** (Windows EH): not used in Jeandle. If
present, any virtual ref reachable through their operands triggers
materialisation.

#### 2.3.16 Store of a virtual ref to non-virtual memory

Technically already covered by 2.3.3 Case C, but emphasised here because
it is the single most common escape path.

**Pattern**: `store ptr addrspace(1) %virtualRef, ptr addrspace(N) %nonVirtualPtr`
where `%nonVirtualPtr` resolves via `stripPointerCastsAndOffsets` to:
- a function argument (parameter — externally visible memory);
- a load from a global variable (static field);
- an already-materialised virtual object's field
  (`State.getObjectState(otherID).MaterializedPtr` + offset);
- any other pointer not tracked as virtual.

**Action**:
- `materialise(virtualRefID)` before the store. The store is preserved.
- Record `Effect::Materialize(ObjID=virtualRefID, Block=currentBlock,
  InsertBefore=SI)`.
- Record `Effect::ReplaceInput(Target=SI, InputIndex=0,
  Replacement=materializedPtr)`.

**Atomic store of a virtual ref to a global** (e.g. via `volatile`):
identical handling. The store's atomicity does not change the escape
status — the reference is published to other threads, which trivially
escapes it.

**Cross-object aliasing**: if the non-virtual base is itself a
materialised pointer of a different virtual object, the store publishes
the virtual ref into that object's heap representation. The receiving
virtual object (if it had been kept virtual via a `VirtualRef` entry)
would have observed the new pointer; but since the receiver is
materialised, its fields live on the heap and the store proceeds
normally after materialising the source virtual ref.

#### 2.3.16a Atomic intrinsics and rare LLVM IR on virtual references

This sub-section enumerates rare-but-legal LLVM IR patterns that touch a
virtual reference, with the disposition for each.

**`freeze ptr addrspace(1) %v`**: returns a non-poison representative
of v. If `%v` resolves to a virtual ObjectID:
- The frozen value is the same SSA identity for our purposes (a virtual
  object's pointer is always non-poison; the allocation invoke returns
  `nonnull dereferenceable`). Add alias:
  `AliasMap::addVirtualAlias(freezeInst, ID, VObj)`.
- Record `Effect::AddVirtualAlias`. The transform pass will replace the
  freeze with the materialised pointer when consumed by an escaping use.

**`bitcast ptr addrspace(1) %v to ptr addrspace(1)`**: identity bitcast.
Add `AliasMap::addVirtualAlias(bc, ID, VObj)`. No effect on identity.

**`bitcast ptr addrspace(1) %v to <other ptr addrspace(1) type>`** (opaque
pointers): with opaque pointers (LLVM 17+), `ptr` has no element type,
so this does not change the value. Handle as identity alias.

**`addrspacecast ptr addrspace(1) %v to ptr addrspace(0)`**: changes
GC tracking semantics. The result is no longer a Java heap reference
from the runtime's perspective. Materialise %v before the cast (the
resulting addrspace(0) pointer can be used in ways that bypass GC, e.g.
passed to native code). Record `Effect::Materialize` and
`Effect::ReplaceInput`.

**`addrspacecast ptr addrspace(0) %v to ptr addrspace(1)`**: synthesises
a Java heap reference from a raw pointer. The result is not a virtual
ref (we did not allocate it). No PEA action on the operand; the result
is not tracked as virtual.

**`ptrtoint ptr addrspace(1) %v to iN`**: launders a virtual ref into an
integer. We conservatively materialise %v at this point so the integer
is the real address. Record `Effect::Materialize` and
`Effect::ReplaceInput`.

**`inttoptr iN %x to ptr addrspace(1)`**: synthesises a heap reference
from an integer. The result is opaque to PEA — not tracked as virtual.
No action on the integer operand; the result is not in the alias map.

**`atomicrmw xchg/add/... ptr addrspace(1) %virtualField, %v`**: handled
in 2.3.3 (`atomicrmw` variant) — always materialise.

**`cmpxchg ptr addrspace(1) %virtualField, %cmp, %new`**: handled in
2.3.3 (`cmpxchg` variant) — fold if compare is statically equal;
materialise otherwise.

**`load atomic %ty, ptr addrspace(1) %virtualField <ordering> <syncscope>`**:
handled in 2.3.4 — atomicity is irrelevant once the load is eliminated.

**`fence <ordering> <syncscope>`**: a memory fence with no operand.
Does not touch any virtual ref. No PEA action.

**`llvm.experimental.guard(i1 %c, ...)`** and similar: not used in
Jeandle. If seen, generic call (2.3.14) — materialise any virtual ref
operand.

**Vector instructions on virtual refs**: `insertelement
<N x ptr addrspace(1)> %vec, ptr addrspace(1) %virtualRef, iN %idx`,
`extractelement <N x ptr addrspace(1)> %vec, iN %idx`,
`shufflevector <N x ptr addrspace(1)> %a, %b, <M x i32> %mask`. v1
action: when a virtual ref flows into a vector operand of any vector
instruction, materialise it (Section 2.3.18 generic escape). Tracking
virtual refs through vector lanes is complex and not worth the
engineering cost for v1.

**Aggregate `insertvalue`/`extractvalue` on virtual refs**: a virtual
ref can be inserted into an `{ ptr addrspace(1), i32 }` aggregate (e.g.
the landingpad result format). When a virtual ref is the value operand
of an `insertvalue`, materialise it.

**`llvm.assume(i1 %c)` with %c involving a virtual ref**: %c is a
boolean. If Tier 3 folds it to true, the assume is a no-op. If folded
to false, the assume is undefined behaviour (leave it; not our job).

**`llvm.invariant.start(i64 %size, ptr addrspace(1) %virtualRef)`**:
marks a memory region as invariant. On a virtual ref, the marker has no
analysable semantics in PEA — treat as no-op. Do not materialise. The
transform pass will dead-code-eliminate it when the allocation is
removed (its token result has no remaining users).

**`llvm.lifetime.start(i64 %size, ptr addrspace(1) %virtualRef)`**:
similar — no-op marker. Do not materialise.

**`llvm.gcroot(ptr, ptr)`**: Jeandle's GC scheme is statepoint-based
(`gc "jeandle-gc"` plus addrspace(1) on heap refs), NOT shadow stack.
`llvm.gcroot` is incompatible; if present, the function has a different
GC strategy and we should skip PEA entirely (the
`java-method-compilation` guard should already exclude such functions).
If seen at PEA, treat as generic escape.

**`call ptr addrspace(1) @llvm.experimental.gc.relocate(...)`**,
**`call ptr addrspace(1) @llvm.experimental.gc.result(...)`**: post-RS4GC
intrinsics. Will not appear at PEA's position. If seen, generic escape.

**Other rare patterns to document but not implement**:
- `catchswitch`, `catchpad`, `cleanuppad`, `catchret`, `cleanupret`
  (Windows-style EH): not used in Jeandle. Skip PEA on functions using
  them.
- `landingpad`: standard itanium-style EH. Result is `{ ptr addrspace(1),
  i32 }`; the `ptr addrspace(1)` is the exception object, allocated
  externally (or freshly by the runtime) — never a virtual object of
  the current method. No PEA action on the landingpad itself.

#### 2.3.17 Select instruction with virtual references

LLVM's `select` has two distinct forms that must be handled separately:

```
%r = select i1 %cond, T1 %tval, T1 %fval               ; scalar form
%r = select <N x i1> %cond, <N x T1> %tv, <N x T1> %fv ; vector form
```

For Jeandle PEA, only `T1 == ptr addrspace(1)` is relevant (i.e., the select produces a Java object reference). Other types are passed through unchanged.

**Vector-conditioned select rule (unconditional materialization).** If the condition is a vector type, the select selects different lanes from `%tv` and `%fv` element-wise. There is no way to assign a single virtual identity to a per-lane mix of virtual references. Therefore: **any virtual operand of a vector-conditioned select must be materialized in the select's parent block before the select, and the operand is replaced with the materialized pointer.** This follows the same materialization-before-use protocol as Section 2.3.18.

**Scalar-conditioned select rule.** Let `T = resolveVirtualRef(%tval)` and `F = resolveVirtualRef(%fval)`. Five cases:

1. **Neither operand is virtual.** The select is irrelevant to PEA; no action. (This case is not reached unless `HasVirtualInputs` falsely marks the select; it must still be checked defensively.)

2. **Both operands resolve to the same `ObjectID == X`, and `X` is still virtual in the current `PEABlockState`.** Make the select an alias for `X`. Record `Effect::AddVirtualAlias(select_result, X)`. No new instruction is created; the select itself will be eliminated by Pass 1 effect application (the select's uses are RAUW'd to the materialized pointer or eliminated when the object is fully virtual). Mark the select as "virtual-input replaced" so downstream uses are reprocessed.

3. **Both operands resolve to ObjectIDs `X` and `Y` with `X != Y`, and both objects are still virtual.** This is the **select-virtualization** case (analogous to Graal's `processPhi` over a two-input synthetic phi). Apply compatibility checks identical to Section 2.3.19's PHI compatibility:
   - Same `VirtualObject::Kind` (both `Instance` or both `Array`).
   - Same `Klass` (instance) or same `ArrayKlass` (array). Because all Jeandle allocations carry `java-klass-exact`, the exact class of `X` must equal the exact class of `Y`.
   - For arrays, same `ArrayLengthVal` (compare by SSA identity, then by ConstantInt value if both are `ConstantInt`). If the lengths are different SSA values that are not provably equal, the field layouts may differ; treat as incompatible.
   - Same `LockCount` in their respective `ObjectState`s.
   - Same `EnsureVirtualized` flag, or both `false`, or downgrade to `false` if not all inputs are `EnsureVirtualized` (mirror Graal's `ensureVirtual &= ...` logic at lines 1399 and 991–994 of `PartialEscapeClosure.java`).
   - **Identity (single-use) check**: `X` and `Y` must each correspond to allocations whose result has exactly one IR use (the select operand). See Section 2.3.19 for the precise rule and Graal's `isSingleUsageAllocation` (PartialEscapeClosure.java:1517–1545).

   If all checks pass: create a new `VirtualObject` via `firstInputVO->duplicate()` (mirroring Graal's `VirtualObjectNode.duplicate()`), assign a fresh `ObjectID = Z`, and run `mergeObjectStates` over a synthetic two-input merge with `States = {currentState, currentState}` and `SourceObjs = {X, Y}` (the same state is used for both "predecessors" because both operands flow within the same basic block). The merge produces field PHI nodes inserted **immediately before the select**, not at any join point, because the select itself is the merge point. The field PHI uses the select's condition replicated: each field PHI is actually realized as a `select i1 %cond, field_T, field_F`. Implementation: instead of `Effect::CreatePHI`, emit `Effect::CreateFieldSelect(cond, tField, fField)`. Make the select an alias for `Z`. Record `Effect::AddVirtualAlias(select_result, Z)`.

   If any check fails: fall through to case 4.

4. **At least one operand is virtual, the other is not (materialized, null, function arg, or a constant ptr).** Materialize the virtual operand(s) in the current block immediately before the select (using `materialize(ObjID, currentBlock, select_inst)`). Replace the select's operand with the materialized pointer via `Effect::ReplaceInput`. The select then operates on two non-virtual pointers and is left in the IR.

5. **Both operands are virtual but one of them has already been materialized in the current state** (e.g., an earlier instruction in the same block escaped it). The virtual one is materialized; the already-materialized one is used as-is. After materialization, the select is a select of two materialized pointers — leave in the IR.

**Detecting virtualizable selects.** A select is processed only if `AliasMap.hasVirtualInputs(select)` is true (i.e., at least one of its operands is in the alias map pointing to a virtual object). Vector-conditioned selects of pointers can be detected by `isa<FixedVectorType>(SI->getCondition()->getType())` — those go straight to materialization regardless. For scalar selects, the four cases above are dispatched by inspecting `resolveVirtualRef` on each arm.

**Recording effects.** All effects for case 3 (field-select creation) are placed in `BlockEffects[selectParentBlock]` with `InsertBefore = select_inst`. The select-virtualization branch is the only place where new `select` instructions are synthesized by PEA (one per differing field).

**Constants and zero-initializers.** A `select i1 %c, ptr addrspace(1) %virtual, ptr addrspace(1) null` falls into case 4: the null is non-virtual, so the virtual side is materialized. Similarly for poison and `addrspacecast`-wrapped null.

#### 2.3.18 Generic case: any instruction with a virtual object input that is NOT a recognized virtualizable pattern

This is the catch-all from Graal's `processNodeInputs` (PartialEscapeClosure.java, indirectly via `ensureMaterialized` calls in `ensureMaterialized` and `processNodeInternal`). For any instruction `I` that has `AliasMap.hasVirtualInputs(I) == true` but is not dispatched by a more specific rule (Sections 2.3.1–2.3.17), the default policy is: **materialize every virtual operand before `I`, then RAUW the operand with the materialized pointer**.

**Dispatch precedence.** Tier-2 dispatch tests rules in this order (first match wins):
1. PHI nodes (Section 2.3.19) — handled inside the merge processor, never reaches the generic case.
2. Select instructions (Section 2.3.17) — has its own specialized logic.
3. Recognized JavaOp calls (Sections 2.3.5–2.3.13) — pattern-matched by callee name.
4. Stores to `ptr addrspace(1)` with a virtual base and constant offset (Section 2.3.3).
5. Loads from `ptr addrspace(1)` with a virtual base and constant offset (Section 2.3.4).
6. Pointer-equality `icmp eq/ne` on `ptr addrspace(1)` (Sections 2.3.11, 2.3.12).
7. `ret` of a `ptr addrspace(1)` (Section 2.3.15).
8. Store of a virtual ref to a non-virtual location (Section 2.3.16).
9. Pointer-typed `freeze`, `bitcast`, `addrspacecast`, and GEP-with-zero-offset — propagate the virtual alias; do not materialize. These are pure pointer-identity-preserving operations and are handled by `resolveVirtualRef` rather than by the dispatcher (they never reach this generic case unless GEP is non-zero, in which case they cascade into the store/load rules above).
10. **All other instructions with virtual inputs** — this section.

**What "operand" means for materialization purposes.** Materialization is triggered by any operand `op` of `I` such that:

- `op->getType()` is `ptr addrspace(1)` (a Java object reference type), AND
- `resolveVirtualRef(op)` returns some `ObjectID` that is still virtual in the current `PEABlockState`.

We deliberately do **not** trigger materialization for:

- `i64` operands that were once `inttoptr`'d from a virtual ref. Such laundering is illegal in Jeandle IR — `ptr addrspace(1)` may only be cast to/from `ptr addrspace(0)` via `addrspacecast`, never via `inttoptr`/`ptrtoint` (which would lose GC tracking). If such a pattern is observed in input IR, treat it as if the integer were opaque and the virtual ref had escaped at the `ptrtoint` site (a separate check upstream of PEA should reject such IR; PEA asserts but does not silently miscompile).
- Metadata-only references (`!alias.scope`, `!noalias`, `!dbg`). Metadata never carries SSA value semantics in a way that can hold an object reference, and Jeandle does not place virtual refs into metadata operands.
- Operand bundles of type `"deopt"`. These are out of scope; deoptimization is excluded by the plan's preamble. Any virtual ref appearing in a deopt bundle is conservatively treated as a materialization trigger to be safe, but in practice the plan assumes deopt bundles do not reference virtual results.

**Operand inspection algorithm.**
```
processGenericInstruction(I, State):
  // Collect all virtual operands first, so materializations happen in a
  // deterministic order (lowest ObjectID first, mirroring Graal's deterministic
  // iteration over virtualObjects).
  virtualOps = []
  for opIdx in 0 .. I->getNumOperands()-1:
    op = I->getOperand(opIdx)
    if (op->getType() != ptr addrspace(1)) continue
    objID = State.resolveVirtualRef(op)
    if (!objID || !State.getObjectState(*objID).isVirtual()) continue
    virtualOps.push_back({opIdx, *objID})

  // Sort by ObjectID for deterministic effect ordering. Same ObjectID may
  // appear in multiple slots — materialize once, replace all such slots.
  std::sort(virtualOps by objID)
  std::unique_by_objid(virtualOps, keeping all slot indices grouped per objID)

  // Materialize each distinct ObjectID once, recursively materializing
  // nested VirtualRef fields.
  for (objID, slots) in virtualOps_grouped:
    materializedPtr = materialize(objID, currentBlock, I)
    for slotIdx in slots:
      record Effect::ReplaceInput(I, slotIdx, materializedPtr) in BlockEffects[currentBlock]
```

**Recursive materialization.** `materialize(objID, block, insertBefore)` walks the object's `Entries`. For any entry that is `FieldValue::VirtualRef(nestedID)`, it first materializes `nestedID` (placed in the same insertion slot, ordered so that nested allocations come first), then stores the materialized pointer into the outer object's field. This is exactly Graal's `materializeWithCommit` recursion (PartialEscapeBlockState.java:292–343). Cycles between virtual objects' field references cannot occur because each `Effect::Materialize` writes one allocation invoke and a sequence of field stores; if `A.f = VirtualRef(B)` and `B.f = VirtualRef(A)`, recursion bottoms out because `escape()` (Graal's term — our equivalent flips `ObjectState::Kind` to `Materialized` in the local clone used by recursion) is performed *before* descending into entries, breaking the cycle on the second visit and causing the second visit to see `B` as already materialized.

**Replacement: `Effect::ReplaceInput`.** This effect records `(Target = I, InputIndex = slotIdx, Replacement = materializedPtr)`. Pass 1 of the transform applies it via `I->setOperand(slotIdx, materializedPtr)`, which updates the SSA def-use chain. The original `ptr addrspace(1)` produced by the allocation invoke is then dead from `I`'s perspective; if all uses of the original allocation are replaced (the allocation is fully virtual), the allocation itself is removed by `Effect::EliminateAllocation` in Pass 2.

**Special case — `ConstantExpr` operands.** LLVM permits `ConstantExpr` (e.g., constant GEPs) as instruction operands. Virtual references in Jeandle are always produced by `invoke @jeandle.new_instance` or `invoke @jeandle.newarray`, which are dynamic instructions, never `Constant`s. Therefore, no `ConstantExpr` operand of any instruction can resolve to a virtual ref. The implementation **asserts** this: `assert(!isa<ConstantExpr>(op) || resolveVirtualRef(op) == std::nullopt)`. If the assert ever fires (e.g., due to a malformed IR test case), it is a bug in the producer, not PEA.

**Special case — `CallBrInst` (asm goto).** Jeandle's IR generator does not emit `callbr`. PEA treats `CallBrInst` like any other call: if any virtual operand is passed, materialize. The successor states (both "indirect" and "default") receive the materialized state (no exception split because `callbr` has no unwind edge in LLVM).

**Special case — vector instructions.** `shufflevector`, `extractelement`, `insertelement` on `<N x ptr addrspace(1)>` materialize all virtual operands. Jeandle does not currently emit such patterns from bytecode, but PEA must remain sound if they appear (e.g., from inlined intrinsics in the future).

**Special case — `atomicrmw xchg` / `cmpxchg` with `ptr addrspace(1)` operands.** Atomic RMW on Java references is treated as a generic escape: materialize the virtual ref (the new-value side) before the RMW. The base pointer side is handled by the store rule (Section 2.3.3) if it's a virtual base; otherwise the value being stored escapes here.

**Order of operands.** Within `processGenericInstruction`, we materialize in ascending `ObjectID` order. The choice is arbitrary but **must be deterministic** so that effect application produces bit-identical output across runs (important for reproducibility and for downstream caching).

**Marking.** After RAUW-replacement of an operand, the instruction `I` is added to `HasScalarReplacedInputs` so that Tier 3 may attempt further simplification (e.g., a `phi` whose all inputs are now the same materialized pointer can be folded).

#### 2.3.19 PHI nodes at merge points

PHI nodes are handled exclusively inside the `MergeProcessor::merge` loop, after object-state merging, before the loop's stability check. This mirrors Graal's `for (PhiNode phi : getPhis())` block at PartialEscapeClosure.java:1041–1046.

A PHI is processed iff `AliasMap.hasVirtualInputs(phi) == true`, i.e., at least one incoming value resolves to a virtual object via the global alias map. (For loop headers, the alias is set by `processInitialLoopState` at the top of loop processing, mirroring Graal lines 723–735.) Other PHIs (no virtual inputs) are left untouched and their alias slot is set to `null` to clear stale state from a prior iteration.

For each processed PHI, we compute the following over its incoming-value/predecessor-block pairs:

```
virtualInputs   = 0
uniqueVirtualObject = true
selfReference   = false
ensureVirtual   = true
virtualObjs[N]  = { nullptr ... }    // one slot per predecessor

For i = 0 .. N-1:
  predValue = phi->getIncomingValue(i)
  predBlock = phi->getIncomingBlock(i)
  alias     = AliasMap.getAlias(predValue)
  if alias is a VirtualObject (i.e., a virtual ObjectID):
    virtualObjs[i] = alias.ObjectID
    objState = PredStates[i].getObjectStateOptional(alias.ObjectID)
    if objState == nullptr:
      // This can only happen when predValue is itself a PHI that hasn't
      // been processed yet. Graal returns false here (PartialEscapeClosure.java:1393).
      bail out — leave the PHI alone, do nothing.
    if objState.isVirtual():
      if virtualObjs[0] != virtualObjs[i]: uniqueVirtualObject = false
      ensureVirtual &= objState.EnsureVirtualized
      virtualInputs++
    // else: materialized — counted as non-virtual input below
  else if alias == phi:
    // Self-reference (loop back-edge)
    assert i > 0    // forward edge can never be a self-reference at this point
    virtualInputs++
    selfReference = true
```

The PHI is then classified:

**Case A — `virtualInputs < N`.** Not all inputs are virtual. Some inputs are non-object (e.g., `null`, function-argument `ptr addrspace(1)`, materialized pointers, or unrecognized constants). Materialize every virtual input in its respective predecessor block, then leave the PHI in the IR with each operand replaced by the materialized pointer (or the original non-virtual value).

For each `i` with `virtualObjs[i] != InvalidObjectID`:
1. If `!ensureVirtual` and the per-predecessor `ObjectState` has `EnsureVirtualized == true`, downgrade it to `false` (Graal lines 1500–1503). This permits materialization even though one branch wanted strict virtualization, because *another* branch has already broken the strict-virtual guarantee.
2. Call `materialize(virtualObjs[i], predBlock[i], predBlock[i]->getTerminator())`. The insertion point is just before the predecessor's terminator. If the predecessor's terminator is itself an `invoke` (i.e., the predecessor is invoke-terminated), insert just before that invoke (Section 2.4.4 elaborates).
3. Record `Effect::SetPHIInput(phi, predIdx = i, value = materializedPtr)` (or for non-virtual inputs, the existing operand). The phi node is **kept** in the IR (we do not `Effect::DeleteNode` it).

Return `materialized = true` if any materialization happened.

**Case B — `virtualInputs == N` AND `uniqueVirtualObject == true`.** All inputs resolve to the *same* `ObjectID`. The PHI is a trivial alias for that object. Record:
- `Effect::AddVirtualAlias(phi, virtualObjs[0])` so that all of `phi`'s users see the same virtual object.
- `Effect::DeleteNode(phi)` (added to `MergeEffects` so that it runs after merging is stable). After deletion, all users of `phi` are redirected to use the canonical alias — but since `phi`'s users are themselves consumers of the virtual object, they will either be eliminated (loads/stores) or escape-materialized.

No materialization happens; return `materialized = false`.

**Case C — `virtualInputs == N` AND `uniqueVirtualObject == false`.** All inputs are virtual but reference *different* `ObjectID`s. This is the **PHI virtualization** path, the heart of PEA's merge logic. Apply compatibility checks (Graal lines 1416–1431):

1. **Type & arity check.** For every `i`, `firstVO->getKind() == virtualObjs[i]->getKind()` and `firstVO->Klass == virtualObjs[i]->Klass` (or `firstVO->ArrayKlass == virtualObjs[i]->ArrayKlass`), and `firstVO->Fields.size() == virtualObjs[i]->Fields.size()`.

2. **Lock equality.** For every `i`, `PredStates[0].getObjectState(firstVO).LockCount == PredStates[i].getObjectState(virtualObjs[i]).LockCount`. Lock identity (the `MonitorIdNode` in Graal) does not exist in Jeandle's flat lock counter — only the count must match.

3. **Identity check (single-use allocation).** For each `i` with a non-null `virtualObjs[i]`:
   - The incoming value `phi->getIncomingValue(i)`, walked through the alias map, must be an allocation result (`invoke @jeandle.new_instance` or `invoke @jeandle.newarray`) — i.e., the actual SSA value that was inserted into the alias map as a `virtualRef`.
   - That allocation must have **exactly one IR use**, which is this PHI input. The check is `incomingValue->hasOneUse() && incomingValue->user_back() == phi`. (Compare Graal's `value instanceof AllocatedObjectNode && value.hasExactlyOneUsage()` at PartialEscapeClosure.java:1524.)
   - Additionally, the predecessor state must reference *only this one virtual object* among the PHI's candidate objects (Graal's `singleVirtual` check at PartialEscapeClosure.java:1531–1543). Implementation: scan `PredStates[i]`'s ObjectStates; for every state in the candidate set `{virtualObjs[0], ..., virtualObjs[N-1]}` that is still virtual and reachable in `PredStates[i]`, only `virtualObjs[i]` is allowed.
   - `selfReference` must be `false`. Self-references add an extra virtual object to the cycle; merging is only sound for objects without identity (Graal's `virtual.hasIdentity()` check at line 1441). Jeandle objects always have identity (no `VirtualBoxingNode` equivalent), so `selfReference == true` ⇒ compatibility fails.

   If any check fails for any `i`, fall through to Case A (materialize all virtual inputs).

4. **Compatibility passed.** Allocate a new merged `VirtualObject`:
   ```
   newVO = virtualObjs[0]->duplicate()    // Detached copy with InvalidObjectID
   Z = newState.createVirtualObject(newVO) // Assigns fresh ObjectID
   ```
   Then construct a per-predecessor `sourceObjects[N] = {virtualObjs[0].ID, ..., virtualObjs[N-1].ID}` (with `selfReference` slots filled per Graal's logic at PartialEscapeClosure.java:1459–1469: on the first iteration use `virtualObjs[0]`, on subsequent iterations use `Z` itself once the new state is established). Call `mergeObjectStates(Z, sourceObjects, PredStates)` to produce field PHIs.

   Record:
   - `Effect::AddVirtualAlias(phi, Z)`.
   - `Effect::AddVirtualAlias(newVO_marker, Z)` — Graal does the equivalent dance with `addVirtualAlias(virtual, virtual)` so that the synthetic VirtualObject node itself can be looked up; in Jeandle we simply ensure the new `ObjectID` is registered in `AliasMap`'s VirtualObject side table.
   - `Effect::DeleteNode(phi)` in `MergeEffects`.

   The PHI is replaced (logically) by the new virtual object `Z`; the original PHI is deleted; field PHIs are placed at the merge block via `mergeObjectStates`.

   Sanity check after merging (Graal lines 1478–1481): if `selfReference` or any intermediate materialization caused `PredStates[i].getObjectState(sourceObjects[i])` to become non-virtual, the merge is **not** compatible; back out, fall through to Case A. This is critical: case-C compatibility can become invalid mid-iteration if a nested field-merge materializes one of the source objects.

**Case D — `virtualInputs > 0` but the bail-out at "`objState == nullptr`" was hit during enumeration.** Leave the PHI as-is for this iteration. The next iteration (if any) will pick it up after the producing PHI is processed.

**Edge cases.**

- **PHI with a single incoming value** (a block with one predecessor — LLVM allows this temporarily after `mem2reg` and prior to SimplifyCFG). Trivially Case B with `N = 1`: the PHI is an alias for the single input's resolution.
- **PHI in a block with all dead predecessors.** The merge block itself is marked dead (Section 2.4 precondition). No PHI processing runs.
- **PHI of PHIs.** RPO processing ensures innermost PHIs are processed first in straight-line code. In loops, the back-edge PHI may have a forward-edge PHI as input that has already been aliased; the back-edge case handles this via `processInitialLoopState` (Graal lines 723–735) which seeds the loop-header PHI's alias from its forward input *before* the loop body is walked.
- **PHI cycle (e.g., `a = phi(init, b); b = phi(a, ...)` in nested loops).** First iteration: forward-edge alias for `a` is `init`'s ObjectID (if virtual). Body processes uses of `a`. Back-edge merge tries to virtualize `a`; depending on `b`'s state at the loop end, either succeeds with a new merged ObjectID or materializes. The do-while stabilization handles transitions.
- **PHI input that is constant `null`.** Resolves to "non-virtual" (null is not a virtual object). Falls into Case A; the virtual inputs of the PHI are materialized.
- **PHI input that is a function argument with `nonnull java-klass-exact` attributes.** Still non-virtual (arguments are pre-existing heap objects). Case A.
- **PHI input from an unreachable predecessor.** During RPO, an unreachable predecessor has no `BlockExitState` (it was never visited because dataflow does not enter it). The merge processor's predecessor-state list excludes any predecessor whose state is `Dead` or missing (Section 2.4 "Phase 0"). The PHI's input slot for that predecessor is left dangling in the IR but ignored by merge math; the transform pass leaves the operand unchanged.
- **Multi-arity PHI (3+ predecessors from a switch).** The compatibility check is N-ary; all `N` virtual objects must be pairwise compatible. Single-use check is per input.
- **Critical-edge PHI input.** A PHI input on a critical edge is handled by inserting the materialization in the predecessor block just before its terminator. Because the predecessor may have multiple successors, the materialization happens *unconditionally* in the predecessor — but PEA already accepts this conservatism (Section 2.4.4).
- **Self-referencing PHI: `phi(init, self)` where the body never modifies `self`.** This is the "trivial loop carrier" pattern. Forward edge contributes `init`'s ObjectID `X`; back edge resolves to `self == phi`, which goes through `getAlias(phi)`. After `processInitialLoopState`, `getAlias(phi) == X` (an aliased VirtualObject). So `virtualObjs[1] == X == virtualObjs[0]` and the PHI hits Case B (uniqueVirtualObject). No materialization.
- **Self-referencing PHI where the loop body mutates the carried object.** Forward edge ObjectID `X`, back edge ObjectID `X` (same VirtualObject, mutated fields). Case B still applies; field mutations are reconciled by `mergeObjectStates` between forward and back edge states.
- **Self-referencing PHI where the back edge produces a *different* ObjectID `Y`.** `uniqueVirtualObject == false` and `selfReference` is set only if the back-edge alias resolves *to the PHI itself*. If it resolves to `Y != phi`, this is Case C (PHI virtualization). The Jeandle identity check then requires both `X` and `Y` to be single-use allocations whose only use is the PHI input — in a loop body that allocates fresh objects per iteration without leaking them, this is the typical "allocation in loop" pattern. If allocations leak (any other use), Case A fires.

**Recording effects.** PHI processing produces effects that go into two buckets:
- `MergeEffects[mergeBB]` — `Effect::CreatePHI` (for merged field PHIs created by `mergeObjectStates`), `Effect::DeleteNode(phi)` (for fully-aliased PHIs in Case B/C).
- `AfterMergeEffects[mergeBB]` — `Effect::SetPHIInput` for each predecessor's contribution to each field PHI.
- `BlockEffects[predBB]` — `Effect::Materialize` for any per-predecessor materialization, plus `Effect::SetPHIInput` for the resulting materialized-pointer PHI (which lives at the merge block and is created by Case A).

The two-bucket merge split mirrors Graal's `mergeEffects` vs `afterMergeEffects` (EffectsClosure.java:644–645). `mergeEffects` runs before `afterMergeEffects` during effect application, ensuring PHI nodes exist before their inputs are written.

#### 2.3.20 InvokeInst exception edge handling

Every `InvokeInst` in Jeandle IR has exactly two successors: a *normal destination* (taken if the callee returns) and an *unwind destination* (taken if the callee throws). PEA splits the analysis state at every invoke, regardless of whether the invoke is an allocation, a JavaOp, or a Java method call.

**Classification of invokes in Jeandle IR.** The dispatcher applies the rules below in order:

1. **Allocation invokes** — `jeandle.new_instance` and `jeandle.newarray`. Result type: `ptr addrspace(1)` with `nonnull`, `java-klass`, `java-klass-exact`. Section 2.3.1–2.3.2 already handles the virtualization side; this section specifies the per-successor state split.
2. **Java method invokes** — `invokevirtual`/`invokespecial`/`invokestatic`/`invokeinterface`/`invokedynamic`/`invokehandle`. The callee is a Java method; **any virtual ref passed as argument escapes**.
3. **JavaOp invokes** (`call_java_op_ex`) — Used when a JavaOp may throw. Currently this is only the two allocations and any future exception-propagating JavaOp.
4. **JavaOps emitted as `call` (not `invoke`)** — `jeandle.checkcast`, `jeandle.instanceof`, `jeandle.arraylength`, `jeandle.load_klass`, `jeandle.idiv`, `jeandle.ldiv`, `jeandle.irem`, `jeandle.lrem`, `jeandle.monitorenter_*`, `jeandle.monitorexit_*`, `jeandle.g1_pre_barrier_loaded`, `jeandle.post_barrier`, `jeandle.array_store_check`, `jeandle.check_if_value_based`, `jeandle.current_thread`, `jeandle.safepoint_poll`. These are **not** InvokeInsts (they are `call` instructions) and do not enter this section. They have a single successor and are handled by the per-instruction Sections 2.3.5–2.3.13 or the generic case (Section 2.3.18).

**Algorithm for an InvokeInst `II` with normal dest `N` and unwind dest `U`.**

```
processInvoke(II, State):
  isAllocation = II is jeandle.new_instance or jeandle.newarray
  isRecognizedNonEscapingJavaOp =
    (II is a JavaOp invoke whose semantics do not escape any arg)
    // Currently empty — all current JavaOps that are emitted as invokes are
    // either allocations (handled above) or escape their arguments.

  if isAllocation:
    // Section 2.3.1 / 2.3.2 has already created the VirtualObject and added
    // it to the alias map. Do NOT materialize arguments (allocations don't
    // escape their klass/length args, which are constants/integers anyway).
    handleAllocationInvoke(II, State, N, U)
    return

  // Non-allocation invoke. Materialize every virtual arg before II.
  for opIdx in 0 .. II->arg_size()-1:
    arg = II->getArgOperand(opIdx)
    if arg->getType() != ptr addrspace(1): continue
    objID = State.resolveVirtualRef(arg)
    if !objID || !State.getObjectState(*objID).isVirtual(): continue
    materializedPtr = materialize(*objID, currentBlock, II)
    record Effect::ReplaceInput(II, opIdx, materializedPtr) in BlockEffects[currentBlock]

  // After materializations, the state's virtual objects have shrunk (the
  // materialized ones are now Materialized in the state).
  NormalState = State.clone()    // COW
  UnwindState = State.clone()    // COW

  // For non-allocation invokes, both successors inherit the same post-call
  // state. Any virtual object that survived (was not used as an arg) is still
  // virtual on both edges.
  // If II returns a ptr addrspace(1) (e.g., a Java method returning an Object),
  // that result is a NEW, fresh, non-virtual ref. It is not added to the
  // alias map. Its attributes (nonnull, java-klass, java-klass-exact if any)
  // are honored by downstream passes.

  publish NormalState as the predecessor exit-state for N
  publish UnwindState as the predecessor exit-state for U
```

**Allocation-invoke split.** For `jeandle.new_instance` and `jeandle.newarray`, the alias added in Section 2.3.1/2.3.2 is **conceptually placed on the normal-edge exit state only**. The unwind edge has no allocation result (the call threw before producing a value). Concretely:

```
handleAllocationInvoke(II, State, N, U):
  // Snapshot the state BEFORE adding the new virtual object.
  PreAllocState = State.clone()

  // Section 2.3.1/2.3.2 added the new ObjectID to State (mutating it).
  // We treat the post-Section-2.3.1 State as the normal-successor state.
  NormalState = State            // already contains the new ObjectID
  UnwindState = PreAllocState    // does NOT contain the new ObjectID

  publish NormalState as the predecessor exit-state for N
  publish UnwindState as the predecessor exit-state for U
```

Because `State` is COW, the snapshot is cheap; only the array entry for the new ObjectID differs between the two clones.

**The new ObjectID's classification on the unwind edge.** Since `UnwindState` does not contain the new ObjectID at all (the ID slot is `null` or out of bounds), any subsequent use of the allocation invoke's result on the unwind path will hit `resolveVirtualRef` returning `std::nullopt`. The unwind path's `landingpad` does not consume the invoke's result; it loads the exception oop from TLS. No virtual ref crosses the exception edge.

**Landingpad and resume.** The unwind successor begins with `landingpad i64` (note: i64 in Jeandle, not `ptr addrspace(1)` — the landingpad type is rewritten to `token` by RS4GC later) and is followed by a TLS load of the exception oop. This load produces a fresh `ptr addrspace(1)` value with `nonnull` attribute — it is **not** a virtual ref. PEA leaves it untouched.

If the function contains a `resume` terminator (uncommon in Jeandle but legal), it is treated as a return-equivalent: any virtual ref reaching `resume` must be materialized (analogous to Section 2.3.15 for `ret`). In current Jeandle IR, `resume` does not appear because `dispatch_exception_to_handler` either calls into a runtime stub or branches to a user-defined catch block.

**`cleanuppad` / `catchswitch` / `catchpad` (Windows EH).** Jeandle is Linux-only; these constructs do not appear. PEA asserts on encountering them so that future ports flag the gap explicitly.

**InvokeInst result attributes.** The normal successor's first instruction (typically a use of the invoke's result) sees a `ptr addrspace(1)` with `nonnull`, `java-klass`, and (for allocations) `java-klass-exact` attributes. These attributes feed `TypeCheckElimination` after PEA runs; PEA itself does not consume them other than to know the exact klass of an allocation, which it already obtained from arg 0 of `jeandle.new_instance`.

**Unreachable invokes.** An `InvokeInst` in an unreachable block has no live `PEABlockState`. The merge processor never visits its successors via this edge.

**Block-self successor.** If, by construction, an invoke's normal or unwind dest is the invoke's own block (extremely unusual; would require self-recursion in basic-block form), the successor is still treated as a separate state. The successor block will then merge two predecessors (itself's forward state, plus the invoke's split state), exercising the standard merge logic.

### 2.4 State Merging at Join Points

When two or more predecessors flow into a merge block, the analysis combines their `PEABlockState`s into a single state for the merge block. The merge is **N-ary** (handles any number of predecessors), **iterative** (a do-while loop re-runs until materializations stabilize), and **effect-recording** (no IR is mutated during merge — only effect lists are populated).

The algorithm mirrors Graal's `MergeProcessor::merge` (PartialEscapeClosure.java:893–1053) combined with `doMergeWithoutDead` (EffectsClosure.java:593–622).

```
merge(PredStates[N], PredBBs[N], MergeBB):

  // --- Phase 0: Filter dead predecessors ---
  alive = []
  stateIndexes = []    // maps alive[i] back to PredBBs[stateIndexes[i]]
  for i in 0 .. N-1:
    if !PredStates[i].isDead():
      stateIndexes.push_back(i)
      alive.push_back(PredStates[i])

  if alive.empty():
    // All predecessors are dead. The merge block is dead too.
    NewState = PredStates[0].clone()
    NewState.setDead()
    return NewState

  if alive.size() == 1:
    // Single live predecessor — degenerate merge. Treat as straight-line:
    // adopt the predecessor's state (COW), and process MergeBB's PHIs as
    // single-input aliases (Case B of Section 2.3.19).
    NewState = adoptStateCOW(alive[0])
    process MergeBB's PHIs in single-input mode
    return NewState

  // --- Phase 1: Iterative merge ---
  NewState = empty PEABlockState
  MergeEffects[MergeBB] = empty effect list
  AfterMergeEffects[MergeBB] = empty effect list

  do {
    materialized = false

    // --- Phase 1a: Identical-state fast path ---
    if PartialEscapeBlockState::identicalObjectStates(alive):
      // All alive predecessors share the same ObjectStates array (literal
      // pointer equality, not deep equality). Adopt it wholesale via
      // adoptObjectStates — no PHIs needed for object state itself, only
      // for live LLVM SSA values.
      NewState.adoptObjectStates(alive[0])
    else:
      // --- Phase 1b: Compute object intersection ---
      intersection = {ObjectID o : ∀i, alive[i].hasObjectState(o)}

      // Objects in some-but-not-all predecessors are implicitly absent
      // from MergeBB's state. They were never created on the missing
      // predecessor's path (e.g., an allocation that happens only on the
      // true-branch). The PHI representation for the allocation's result,
      // if used after the merge, will have been materialized in the
      // predecessor that has the virtual object — see Phase 1c.
      //
      // Specifically: any ObjectID in (∪ - ∩) is *not* added to NewState.
      // The PEA semantics is: after the merge, that ObjectID is not
      // reachable. The merge block cannot reference it as a virtual ref;
      // any later use must go through a PHI of materialized pointers
      // (which is the natural behavior of LLVM IR after the merge anyway,
      // since the allocation's def doesn't dominate the merge).

      // --- Phase 1c: Per-object merge ---
      for objID in intersection (sorted ascending for determinism):

        // --- Phase 1c.i: Per-object identical-state fast path ---
        if PartialEscapeBlockState::identicalObjectStates(alive, objID):
          NewState.addObject(objID, alive[0].getObjectState(objID).share())
          continue

        // --- Phase 1c.ii: Compute per-predecessor properties ---
        virtualCount = 0
        locksMatch = true
        ensureVirtual = true
        startObj = alive[0].getObjectState(objID)
        uniqueMaterializedValue = startObj.isVirtual()
                                  ? nullptr
                                  : startObj.MaterializedPtr
        for i in 0 .. alive.size()-1:
          obj = alive[i].getObjectState(objID)
          ensureVirtual &= obj.EnsureVirtualized
          if obj.isVirtual():
            virtualCount++
            uniqueMaterializedValue = nullptr
            locksMatch &= (obj.LockCount == startObj.LockCount)
          else if obj.MaterializedPtr != uniqueMaterializedValue:
            uniqueMaterializedValue = nullptr

        // --- Phase 1c.iii: All-virtual, locks match ---
        if virtualCount == alive.size() && locksMatch:
          // Try to keep the object virtual; recurse into field merging.
          // mergeObjectStates may return materialized=true if a nested
          // VirtualRef field could not be merged.
          materialized |= mergeObjectStates(objID, /*sourceObjs=*/null, alive)
          continue

        // --- Phase 1c.iv: All materialized to the same pointer ---
        if uniqueMaterializedValue != nullptr:
          NewState.addObject(objID, ObjectState::materialized(
                                       uniqueMaterializedValue,
                                       LockCount = 0,
                                       EnsureVirtualized = ensureVirtual))
          continue

        // --- Phase 1c.v: Mixed or distinct materialized — PHI ---
```
