| `invoke @jeandle.newarray(arrayKlass, length)` | called fn name == `jeandle.newarray`; `length` must be `ConstantInt` ≤ `JeandleArrayVirtualizationLimit` (default 32) to virtualize | Create VirtualObject of array type, ObjectState size = N, all elements Unknown | Same as new_instance | Same as new_instance | NeverEscapes / PartiallyEscapes / AlwaysEscapes (non-const length → always) |
| `%slot = getelementptr inbounds i8, ptr addrspace(1) %V, i64 K` followed by `store %x, %slot` | base resolves via alias map to virtual `V_k`; offset K is `ConstantInt` | Update `V_k.Fields[K] = Scalar(%x)` (or `VirtualRef(j)` if `%x` resolves to virtual `V_j`); record `EliminatedStore` | Erase GEP + store (in pass 2) | `EraseInstruction` (×2) | none (write doesn't escape) |
| `%slot = GEP %V, K; %v = load %slot` | base virtual, offset constant | Look up `V_k.Fields[K]`; record `LoadReplacement(%v → fieldValue)` | RAUW `%v` with fieldValue, then erase load + GEP | `ReplaceUsesWith`, `EraseInstruction` (×2) | none |
| `GEP %V, non-constant-offset` | base virtual, offset non-constant | Force materialization of `V_k` | Insert materialization, leave GEP alone (now targets materialized pointer via RAUW of base) | `InsertMaterialization`, `ReplaceUsesWith` (base) | PartiallyEscapes |
| `store %V, %nonVirtualSlot` (storing a virtual ref into a non-virtual location) | RHS resolves to virtual, LHS does not | Force materialization of `V_k` before this store | Insert materialization before store; RAUW `%V` → materialized ptr; leave store intact | `InsertMaterialization`, `ReplaceUsesWith` | PartiallyEscapes |

### 4.2 Locks

| Pattern | Detection Rule | Analysis Action | Transform Action | Effect Kinds Used | Escape Outcome |
|---------|---------------|-----------------|------------------|-------------------|----------------|
| `call i1 @jeandle.monitorenter_with_{monitor,thin,lightweight}_lock(obj, slot)` | called-fn match; `obj` resolves to virtual | `V_k.LockCount++`; record `(MonitorEnterFn, slot)` in `V_k.Locks`; record `EliminatedLock` | RAUW i1 → true; erase call; later `br i1 true` simplifies | `ReplaceUsesWith`, `EraseInstruction`, `SimplifyConditionalBranch` | none |
| `call i1 @jeandle.monitorexit_with_*(obj, slot)` | called-fn match; obj virtual | `V_k.LockCount--`; record `EliminatedLock` | Same as enter | Same | none |
| Lock on virtual obj that later escapes | enter virtual, escape downstream | Record lock state on the VirtualObject; on materialization, re-emit lock calls in the materialization block | `InsertMaterialization` (with Locks list), `EraseInstruction` (orig enter/exit) | PartiallyEscapes |
| Unbalanced enter/exit (LockCount < 0 or > 0 at function exit) | analysis tracks per-state | Force materialization (cannot virtualize) | Materialize immediately | `InsertMaterialization` | AlwaysEscapes |

### 4.3 Type checks and reflection

| Pattern | Detection Rule | Analysis Action | Transform Action | Effect Kinds Used | Escape Outcome |
|---------|---------------|-----------------|------------------|-------------------|----------------|
| `call i32 @jeandle.arraylength(obj)` | obj virtual array | RAUW with tracked `ConstantInt` length | RAUW + erase | `ReplaceUsesWith`, `EraseInstruction` | none |
| `call ptr addrspace(0) @jeandle.load_klass(obj)` | obj virtual; klass known | Replace with `inttoptr (i64 KlassEnc to ptr)` | RAUW + erase | Same | none |
| `call i1 @jeandle.checkcast(super, obj)` | obj virtual, exact klass known; `IsSubtype(klass, super)` via VMCallbacks | Replace with `true` (compatible) or `false` (incompatible) | RAUW + erase; simplify branch | `ReplaceUsesWith`, `EraseInstruction`, `SimplifyConditionalBranch` | none |
| `call i1 @jeandle.check_instanceof(super, obj)` | Same as checkcast | Same | Same | Same | none |
| `call i32 @jeandle.instanceof(super, obj)` | Same | Replace with `0` or `1` | Same | Same | none |
| `call i1 @jeandle.check_if_value_based(obj)` | obj virtual | If value-based klass: force materialization (per JLS); else replace with `false` | `InsertMaterialization` OR `ReplaceUsesWith`+`EraseInstruction` | PartiallyEscapes / none |
| `call void @jeandle.array_store_check(value, array)` | array virtual | Does not cause escape *of the array*. Stored value's escape status is tracked separately. | Leave call as-is; analysis only updates element types | (none) | none |

### 4.4 Identity, null, and comparison

| Pattern | Detection Rule | Analysis Action | Transform Action | Effect Kinds Used | Escape Outcome |
|---------|---------------|-----------------|------------------|-------------------|----------------|
| `icmp eq/ne %obj, null` | obj virtual | Virtual ⇒ never null. Fold to `false`/`true`. | RAUW + erase; simplify branch | `ReplaceUsesWith`, `EraseInstruction`, `SimplifyConditionalBranch` | none |
| `icmp eq %a, %b` (both virtual, same V_k) | aliases match | Fold to `true` | Same | Same | none |
| `icmp eq %a, %b` (both virtual, different V_k) | distinct aliases | Fold to `false` (each `new` has distinct identity) | Same | Same | none |
| `icmp eq %virtual, %nonVirtual` | one alias only | Fold to `false` (virtual is fresh-allocated) | Same | Same | none |
| `select i1 %c, %a, %b` (one or both virtual) | RHS / LHS virtual | Same logic as PHI (Section 4.6) | Same as PHI | Various | varies |

### 4.5 Calls and control transfers

| Pattern | Detection Rule | Analysis Action | Transform Action | Effect Kinds Used | Escape Outcome |
|---------|---------------|-----------------|------------------|-------------------|----------------|
| `call/invoke @foo(%V, ...)` where `%V` resolves to virtual and `foo` is not in the recognized-virtualizable list | callee not virtualizable | Materialize `V_k` before this call | `InsertMaterialization`, `ReplaceUsesWith` (the `%V` arg) | PartiallyEscapes |
| `ret %V` | `%V` resolves to virtual | Materialize before ret | Same as call | AlwaysEscapes (effectively) |
| `resume` with virtual ref in tracked state | rare | Materialize | Same | AlwaysEscapes |
| `invoke` exception edges | normal vs unwind | The virtual object's state at the normal-dest entry is the post-call state; the unwind dest gets the *pre-call* state (the call may have failed before any side effect). | State-split at the invoke | (state mgmt only) | n/a |
| `call @jeandle.g1_pre_barrier(obj)` / `g1_post_barrier(addr, val)` | obj/val virtual | PEA runs *before* `InsertGCBarriers`, so these never appear. Defensive: if they do, materialize. | Defensive `InsertMaterialization` | n/a (precluded by ordering) |

### 4.6 Merges (PHI / select)

| Pattern | Detection Rule | Analysis Action | Transform Action | Effect Kinds Used | Escape Outcome |
|---------|---------------|-----------------|------------------|-------------------|----------------|
| `phi [%a, %P1], [%b, %P2]` all incomings alias same V_k | all alias to same id | Add PHI to alias map (`alias[PHI] = V_k`); no IR change | `RecordAliasMapping` | none |
| `phi` incomings alias *different* V_k of *compatible* type and identity unobservable | identity test = single-use allocation; types match; locks compatible | Create merged V_m; for each field where states differ, emit `CreatePHI` + N `SetPHIIncoming`; alias[PHI] = V_m | `CreatePHI`, `SetPHIIncoming` (×N), `RecordAliasMapping` | none |
| `phi` incomings have *incompatible* virtual states | types differ, identity observable, or lock states mismatch | Materialize all virtual inputs in their predecessor blocks; emit `CreatePHI` over materialized pointers | `InsertMaterialization` (×N), `CreatePHI`, `SetPHIIncoming` (×N) | PartiallyEscapes (per input) |
| `phi` mixes virtual and materialized incomings | one alias is virtual, one is a real pointer | Materialize the virtual incoming(s) in their pred block; emit standard PHI | Same as incompatible | PartiallyEscapes |

### 4.7 Less common patterns (defensive handling)

| Pattern | Detection Rule | Analysis Action | Transform Action | Effect Kinds Used | Escape Outcome |
|---------|---------------|-----------------|------------------|-------------------|----------------|
| `atomicrmw <op> %slot, %x` where `%slot` GEPs into virtual | LHS resolves to virtual field | Force materialization (atomic semantics require a real memory location) | `InsertMaterialization`, `ReplaceUsesWith` | PartiallyEscapes |
| `cmpxchg %slot, %cmp, %new` on virtual field | same | Same | Same | PartiallyEscapes |
| `call @llvm.memcpy.p1.p1.i64(%dst, %src, %n, ...)` where dst or src resolves to virtual | virtual ptr operand | Force materialization of any virtual operand | `InsertMaterialization`, `ReplaceUsesWith` | PartiallyEscapes |
| `call @llvm.memset.p1.i64(%dst, %v, %n, ...)` | dst virtual | If `%n` is `ConstantInt` and covers a known-prefix of fields: set those fields to scalar(`%v` zero-extended); else materialize | `EraseInstruction` OR `InsertMaterialization` | none / PartiallyEscapes |
| `call @llvm.memmove.p1.p1.i64(...)` | same | Same as memcpy | Same | PartiallyEscapes |
| `call @llvm.lifetime.{start,end}(...)` on virtual | uncommon (Jeandle uses heap, not stack, for objects) | Ignore (intrinsic is informational) | Leave as-is; will get removed when underlying alloca is erased | (none) | none |
| `call @llvm.assume(i1 %cond)` referencing virtual | the cond may reference a load that is being eliminated | RAUW the load reference; if the assume becomes `assume(true)`, drop it; if `assume(false)`, leave (unreachable behavior) | `ReplaceUsesWith`, possibly `EraseInstruction` | none |
| `ptrtoint ptr addrspace(1) %V to i64` | operand virtual | Force materialization (identity-as-integer escapes) | `InsertMaterialization`, `ReplaceUsesWith` | PartiallyEscapes |
| `inttoptr i64 %x to ptr addrspace(1)` resulting in possible alias to virtual | analysis-side: never aliases a virtual (fresh allocations are not addressable as integers) | No action | (none) | n/a |
| `freeze %V` where V is virtual | operand virtual | Treat as alias-preserving (`alias[freeze] = alias[V]`) | If freeze becomes pure passthrough after RAUW: erase | `RecordAliasMapping`, possibly `EraseInstruction` | none |
| `extractvalue`/`insertvalue` on aggregate-typed virtual (rare — Jeandle objects are pointer-typed, not struct-typed; this arises only if a frontend pass synthesized a struct) | aggregate operand resolves to virtual | Treat as field access at the indexed offset | `EraseInstruction` (the extract/insert), `ReplaceUsesWith` | none |
| `addrspacecast ptr addrspace(1) %V to ptr addrspace(0)` | operand virtual | Conservative: force materialization (cross-address-space code may store, lock, or compare) | `InsertMaterialization`, `ReplaceUsesWith` | PartiallyEscapes |
| Unreachable code containing virtual uses | block is unreachable per CFG analysis | Skip block; do not analyze | (none) | n/a |

---


---

## 5. Key Design Decisions

This section summarizes the load-bearing decisions that shape the implementation. Each decision is cross-referenced to the structures in Section 1 and to the Graal source code it follows (or, where we depart, explains the departure).

### 5.1 Two-pass design (analysis + transform) with per-block effect recording

**Rationale**: LLVM's pass manager requires analysis passes to be read-only. Separating analysis from transformation also follows LLVM conventions, enables result caching, and makes the analysis composable with other passes. We additionally adopt Graal's key architectural insight: *all* transformations are recorded as deferred `Effect`s during analysis, never applied immediately (Section 1.6). This enables:
- **Correct placement**: Materialization Effects carry `InsertBefore` and `Block`, so the transform inserts them at exactly the right IR position in exactly the right predecessor block, mirroring Graal's `PartialEscapeBlockState.materializeBefore` (`PartialEscapeBlockState.java:209`).
- **Effect discarding**: Loop non-convergence calls `clearBlockEffects(loopBlocks)` to throw away an iteration's tentative IR-mutating decisions without touching IR (Section 1.6, mirroring `EffectList.clear`, `EffectList.java:209`).
- **Ordered application**: Effects are applied in RPO + `SeqNo` order, in two passes (non-CFG-kill first, then CFG-kill), mirroring Graal's `Effect.isCfgKill()` partitioning (`EffectList.java:217-236`).
- **Strict separation**: Effects are a *pure declarative record*. The analysis pass MUST NOT consult `PEAResult::BlockEffects` to make further decisions.

### 5.2 PEA runs before JavaOperationLower(0)

**Rationale**: Phase-0 JavaOps (`jeandle.monitorenter_*`, `jeandle.monitorexit_*`, `jeandle.checkcast`, `jeandle.instanceof`, `jeandle.arraylength`, `jeandle.load_klass`) must be identifiable as distinct call sites for PEA to fold them. Phase-1 JavaOps (`jeandle.new_instance`, `jeandle.newarray`) carry the klass and size operands PEA needs to construct `VirtualObject` (Section 1.1). After lowering, these calls are inlined into their helper implementations and the operands are buried under fast-path/slow-path branches, making pattern recognition impractical. Running PEA first preserves the clean call-site form on which all of Section 4's pattern table relies.

### 5.3 No deoptimization

**Rationale**: Per project requirements. We do *not* maintain frame states, deopt bundles, `EscapeObjectState`, `VirtualObjectState`, or `MaterializedObjectState` nodes. The `"deopt"()` operand bundles on `InvokeInst`s (created at e.g. `jeandleAbstractInterpreter.cpp:2763`) are ignored by PEA. Where Graal's analysis would record a virtual object's state into a `FrameState` for deopt reconstruction (see `GraphEffectList.addVirtualMapping`, `GraphEffectList.java:210`; `ObjectState.createEscapeObjectState`, `ObjectState.java:139`), we omit the corresponding Effect entirely.

This is a *substantial* simplification: it removes the entire `EscapeObjectState`/`VirtualObjectState`/`MaterializedObjectState` family from the design, removes `cachedState` from `ObjectState` (compare `ObjectState.java:59`), and removes `AddVirtualMapping` / `UpdateVirtualMapping` from the Effect enum (compare `GraphEffectList.java:210, 245`).

The consequence is that a method compiled with PEA produces correct user-visible behavior but cannot deoptimize to interpreter frames with the correct heap state. For Jeandle's current scope (no deopt), this is acceptable.

### 5.4 Lazy field discovery

**Rationale**: The JVM's field layout is not available inside the LLVM pass — the frontend has already lowered `getfield`/`putfield` to GEP+load/store using `compute_instance_field_address` (`jeandleAbstractInterpreter.cpp:2419`) and array access to `base + index*scale` GEPs (`compute_array_element_address`, `jeandleAbstractInterpreter.cpp:2519`). PEA reverses this by observing GEP offsets at use time and entering them into `VirtualObject::Fields` lazily (Section 1.1). Non-constant offsets or unrecognized GEP shapes cause immediate materialization. The type-coercion policy (Section 1.1 design note) addresses the corner case of multiple stores at the same offset.

### 5.5 Address space(1) as Java heap filter

**Rationale**: Only `ptr addrspace(1)` (`jeandle::AddrSpace::JavaHeapAddrSpace`, `Metadata.h:32`) can reference Java heap objects. `addrspace(0)` is the C heap (used by Jeandle for VM-data structures and OOP-handle indirections); `addrspace(2)` is TLS. PEA filters every candidate Value through an address-space check (Invariant V5, Section 1.7), ensuring we never virtualize a pointer that is not actually a Java heap pointer.

### 5.6 Bounded loop iteration with effect discarding

**Rationale**: Following Graal's design, when a loop iteration does not converge, the per-block analysis effects recorded during that iteration MUST be discarded before re-iterating, because effects from a failed iteration would be based on stale state (Section 1.6). Implementation: `PEAResult::clearBlockEffects(LoopBlocks)` clears the appropriate buckets; the new iteration re-populates them. A fixed bound of 10 iterations applies, with conservative materialization as a fallback.

### 5.7 PHI virtualization with single-usage-allocation check

**Rationale**: When different `VirtualObject`s flow into a PHI, Graal allows merging them into a single new `VirtualObject` if (a) they have compatible layouts and (b) their identity is unobservable. We adopt the conservative single-PHI-use approximation (`VirtualObject::IsSingleUsageAllocation`, Section 1.1). The merged object is created via `VirtualObject::duplicate()` + `createVirtualObject()` to obtain a fresh ObjectID (Invariant V2, Section 1.7), and field values are merged via `CreatePHI` + `SetPHIInput` effects (Section 1.6). When the precondition fails, all virtual inputs are materialized in their respective predecessor blocks.

### 5.8 Iterative merge with materialization stabilization

**Rationale**: The merge at join points may iterate, because materializing one object during merge can invalidate previous merge decisions for *other* objects whose `Entries` referenced the now-materialized one (Invariant V4, Section 1.7). On each re-iteration the merged `ObjectStates` and `MergeEffects[MergeBB]` are cleared via `clearMergeEffects()` (Section 1.6); the predecessor-block materializations (in `BlockEffects[PredBB]`) are *preserved* because they are valid regardless. This mirrors Graal's `do { ... } while (materialized)` pattern in `MergeProcessor.merge()`.

### 5.9 Copy-on-write on PEABlockState (two-level)

**Rationale**: The two-level COW design (Section 1.4 design note) follows Graal's `PartialEscapeBlockState` exactly: vector-level refcount (`PartialEscapeBlockState.java:77, 147`) plus per-`ObjectState` COW flag (`ObjectState.java:62-65, 323`). For the initial implementation we may use full deep cloning at branch points (expected `< 10` virtual objects per function makes this cheap), but the data structure already exposes `markShared()` / `getObjectStateForModification()` / `adoptObjectStates()` so the optimization can be turned on later without touching call sites.

### 5.10 Global alias map (not per-block)

**Rationale**: Section 1.5 design note. The alias map is a function-wide table because (a) aliases stay valid at every dominated use once established, and (b) loop re-iteration may *change* an alias and we want `resetAlias()` on entry to affected blocks to be cheap. Per-block alias maps would require cloning the map at every CFG fork and merging at every join, multiplying work by the number of basic blocks. Graal makes exactly this choice via `EffectsClosure.aliases` as a single global `NodeMap`.

### 5.11 Read elimination not included in initial implementation

**Rationale**: Graal's `PEReadEliminationClosure` adds ~50% more implementation surface on top of core PEA. The standalone `ReadEliminationPhase` exists independently in Graal and can be added as a future extension. The initial Jeandle PEA focuses on getting core escape analysis, scalar replacement, and materialization correct first. Section 1.2's `FieldValue` data structure is read-elimination-ready: the analysis populates `Entries` from stores, and `Scalar`-tagged entries are load-forwarded to satisfy subsequent loads on the same virtual object; extending this to *materialized* objects is the future work.

### 5.12 Three-tier processing model

**Rationale**: Following Graal's `processNodeInternal`, the three-tier model ensures that:
- Tier 1 (allocations) is always processed, regardless of whether there are virtual inputs.
- Tier 2 (virtual inputs, gated by `AliasMap::hasVirtualInputs`) handles known virtualizable patterns and materializes unknown patterns.
- Tier 3 (scalar-replaced inputs, gated by `AliasMap::hasScalarReplacedInputs`) enables further simplification after Tier 2 has replaced virtual references with concrete values.

This tiered approach avoids the "big switch statement" anti-pattern of HotSpot server compiler's `escape.cpp`, where all node types are handled in one centralized function.

### 5.13 Separate ObjectID space (rather than using Value* as identity)

**Rationale**: It is tempting to use the allocation `CallBase*` as the virtual object identity (one Value, one virtual object). This fails for two reasons:

1. **PHI virtualization creates new virtual objects with no Value origin.** When two different allocations flow into a PHI and we virtualize the merge (Section 5.7), the resulting *merged* virtual object has no single Value* that represents it — it is a *new* virtual object whose "address" is the PHI. Using the PHI as the identity would conflate the merged virtual object with the original allocations, which is wrong.

2. **The same allocation site may produce multiple virtual objects during loop re-iteration.** Although Jeandle's frontend emits each allocation call once, loop-iterating analysis may need to *re-virtualize* an allocation under different states. A separate `ObjectID` space lets us distinguish these without ambiguity. Graal makes the same choice with `VirtualObjectNode.objectId` being a separate field from the Node's identity (`VirtualObjectNode.java:52`).

`ObjectID` is therefore a small unsigned integer assigned by `PEAResult::createVirtualObject` and never reused (Invariant V2). The `AliasMap` provides the `Value*` -> `ObjectID` mapping where one exists.

### 5.14 LLVM IR vs Graal graph: structural differences and consequences

Graal operates on a structured graph where each operation is a `Node`, and control-flow joins are `MergeNode`s with associated `PhiNode`s as graph nodes. LLVM IR has a clearer separation: basic blocks of `Instruction`s with PHIs as the first instructions, and CFG edges between blocks. The consequences for PEA design:

- **Effects are per-block, not per-node**: In Graal, an effect can be attached to any `Node`. In LLVM, the natural unit for ordered application is the BasicBlock. We bucket Effects by `BasicBlock*` and apply them in RPO (Section 1.6 deterministic-ordering note).

- **PHI semantics differ**: LLVM PHIs are positional (`i`-th incoming value comes from the `i`-th incoming block); Graal's PhiNodes are positionally tied to the MergeNode's predecessors. When we kill a predecessor edge (CFG-kill effect `KillBranch` or `DeleteNode`), we must update PHI input indices in successor blocks. Our two-pass effect application ordering (non-CFG-kill first, then CFG-kill) ensures PHIs are first patched to their final operands and then their input lists are compacted.

- **Floating nodes vs instructions**: Graal has "floating" nodes (e.g., `AllocatedObjectNode`) that are not yet placed in the schedule and get attached during effect application (`GraphEffectList.addFloatingNode`, `GraphEffectList.java:154`). LLVM instructions are always placed. Our `Materialize` effect therefore always carries `InsertBefore`, and the transform creates the allocation call directly at that point.

- **No "node identity" beyond pointer equality**: Graal's `VirtualizerTool.getAlias` returns the canonical ValueNode for a given alias by leaning on Graal's graph uniqueing. LLVM has no equivalent uniqueing for general instructions, so our `AliasMap` is the sole source of alias truth.

- **Operand bundles**: LLVM `CallBase` operand bundles (`"deopt"`, `"gc-live"`) do not appear in Graal. PEA ignores `"deopt"` (Section 5.3); `"gc-live"` is added later (after PEA) by RewriteStatepointsForGC and is therefore not present during analysis.

### 5.15 Idempotency: PEA tolerates being run multiple times

**Rationale**: PEA is structured as a stand-alone pass. The pass pipeline may legitimately run it more than once (e.g., experimentally, before and after inlining if we later support inlining). Idempotency rules:

1. **A materialized object cannot be re-virtualized**: Once an allocation has been materialized (because a previous PEA run determined escape), the resulting allocation IR is a real `InvokeInst` to `jeandle.new_instance` / `jeandle.newarray` — indistinguishable from a user-written allocation. A subsequent PEA run will rediscover and re-virtualize it *only if* the new analysis finds non-escape. This is sound because PEA's escape determination is purely flow-sensitive on current IR.

2. **No state survives between runs**: `PEAResult` is regenerated from scratch on each analysis run. No cached `ObjectID`s persist across runs. The `AliasMap` is local to a single analysis run.

3. **Transform is a no-op when there are no opportunities**: If `PEAResult::hasOptimizationOpportunity() == false`, the transform pass exits without touching IR. Two consecutive PEA runs on a stable IR therefore reach a fixed point after one transformative run + one confirmation run.

### 5.16 Order of effect application is deterministic

**Rationale**: Detailed in Section 1.6 design note. Three sources of order: (a) RPO of basic blocks, computed once via `ReversePostOrderTraversal<Function*>` for stable iteration; (b) `SeqNo` within a block, assigned in `add*Effect` call order; (c) the non-CFG-kill-then-CFG-kill two-pass split. Together these guarantee bit-identical IR output across runs on the same input. This is essential for FileCheck-style tests in `jeandle-llvm/llvm/test/Jeandle/`.

### 5.17 Effects do not mutate analysis state

**Rationale**: Detailed in Section 1.6 design note. Adding an `Effect` is a pure record-keeping operation; it must NOT change `PEABlockState`, `ObjectState`, `AliasMap`, or any other analysis-time data. The analysis performs every state update via the `PEABlockState`/`AliasMap` mutator APIs *first*, then records the corresponding `Effect` *second*. This ordering ensures that if `addBlockEffect` is later optimized to a no-op (e.g., to dry-run the analysis for benchmarking), the analysis would still produce the same `PEABlockState`s.

### 5.18 Attribute and metadata preservation on Load replacement

**Rationale**: Loads in Jeandle-generated IR routinely carry rich metadata: `!java-klass` and `!java-klass-exact` (`jeandleAbstractInterpreter.cpp:2391-2395, 2587-2591`), `!nonnull`, `!range`, `!align`, and TBAA. When PEA replaces a Load with a scalar value, the metadata on the original Load must NOT be lost because downstream passes (`TypeCheckElimination`, `O3`, `InsertGCBarriers`) rely on it. Section 1.7 Invariant V6 specifies the preservation rules.

The Jeandle attributes `java-klass` / `java-klass-exact` are *parameter return attributes* on call sites (`Attributes.h:29-31`) — they apply to `CallBase` return values, not Loads. When a `ReplaceCall` effect replaces a `jeandle.load_klass` call with a constant Klass pointer, the attribute is not needed on the constant. When it replaces a `jeandle.checkcast` invoke with its argument, the argument's existing attributes already convey the type.

### 5.19 Thread-safety: pass-local analysis only

**Rationale**: PEA runs as a per-`Function` analysis under the LLVM `FunctionAnalysisManager`. The analysis instance is owned by the pass manager; all data structures (`PEAResult`, `AliasMap`, `PEABlockState`) are accessed by a single thread for the duration of one `PartialEscapeAnalysis::run`. We do NOT need locking, atomic counters, or TLS-anywhere. The `NextSeqNo` counter in `PEAResult` is a plain `uint32_t`. If LLVM's `FunctionAnalysisManager` is later extended to parallelize across functions, each function gets its own `PEAResult` so the per-function invariants remain intact.

---

## 6. Implementation Steps

The implementation is structured into ten phases. Each phase is independently testable, builds on its predecessors, and gates on a small set of deliverables. The total estimate is ~16-20 weeks of focused engineering. Time estimates assume a single engineer familiar with LLVM passes; reduce proportionally for parallel work.

### Phase 1: Scaffolding (1 week)

**Goal**: pass classes exist, register in the pipeline, run as no-ops, and produce a valid (unchanged) IR.

**Deliverables**:
- `llvm/include/llvm/Transforms/Jeandle/PartialEscapeAnalysis.h` declaring `PartialEscapeAnalysis : AnalysisInfoMixin<PartialEscapeAnalysis>` with `using Result = PEAResult;` and `Result run(Function&, FunctionAnalysisManager&)` returning an empty `PEAResult`.
- `llvm/include/llvm/Transforms/Jeandle/PartialEscapeTransform.h` declaring `PartialEscapeTransform : PassInfoMixin<PartialEscapeTransform>` with `PreservedAnalyses run(Function&, FunctionAnalysisManager&)` returning `PreservedAnalyses::all()`.
- `llvm/lib/Transforms/Jeandle/PartialEscapeAnalysis.cpp` and `PartialEscapeTransform.cpp` with empty `run` bodies.
- Pass registration: in `llvm/lib/Passes/PassRegistry.def` add (under the function-analysis section):
  ```
  FUNCTION_ANALYSIS("partial-escape-analysis", PartialEscapeAnalysis())
  FUNCTION_PASS("partial-escape-transform", PartialEscapeTransform())
  ```
- CMake: add `PartialEscapeAnalysis.cpp` and `PartialEscapeTransform.cpp` to `llvm/lib/Transforms/Jeandle/CMakeLists.txt`.
- Pipeline: in `Pipeline.cpp` insert before `JavaOperationLower(0)`:
  ```cpp
  PM.addPass(createModuleToFunctionPassAdaptor(RequireAnalysisPass<PartialEscapeAnalysis, Function>()));
  PM.addPass(createModuleToFunctionPassAdaptor(PartialEscapeTransform()));
  ```

**Tests**:
- `llvm/test/Jeandle/partial-escape/scaffolding.ll`: a trivial allocate-and-return test, run with `-passes="require<partial-escape-analysis>,partial-escape-transform"`, FileCheck-verify the IR is unchanged.

**Files modified**:
- `Pipeline.cpp`, `PassRegistry.def`, `CMakeLists.txt`, new headers/sources.

**Dependencies**: none.

### Phase 2: Data Structures (1-2 weeks)

**Goal**: in-memory representation of virtual objects and effects, with full unit-test coverage.

**Deliverables**:
- `llvm/include/llvm/IR/Jeandle/PartialEscape.h`: `VirtualObjectId`, `FieldValue` (variant: Unknown/Scalar/VirtualRef/MaterializedRef), `ObjectState`, `VirtualObject`, `PEABlockState`, `PEAResult`, `AliasMap`.
- `llvm/include/llvm/IR/Jeandle/PartialEscapeEffects.h`: `EffectKind` enum, `Effect` struct (a discriminated union), `EffectList` (thin wrapper over `SmallVector<Effect, 16>`).
- `llvm/lib/Transforms/Jeandle/PartialEscapeUtils.cpp`: helper functions (`resolveVirtualRef`, `isVirtualizableAllocation`, `extractConstantFieldOffset`, `getFunctionByName`, `findOrSynthesizeUnwindDest`).
- `PEABlockState` clone (copy-on-write), `merge`, `markEscaped`.
- `AliasMap` get/set/clear with appropriate `DenseMap` storage and HasVirtualInputs / HasScalarReplacedInputs side-band bitvectors.

**Tests** (`llvm/unittests/Jeandle/PartialEscapeTest.cpp`):
- `FieldValueRoundtripTest`, `ObjectStateCloneTest`, `MergeIdentityTest`, `MergeIncompatibleTest`, `AliasMapBasicTest`, `EffectListOrderTest`.

**Files modified**: new headers + new source + `llvm/unittests/Jeandle/CMakeLists.txt`.

**Dependencies**: Phase 1.

### Phase 3: Forward Dataflow without Loops (2 weeks)

**Goal**: PEA correctly virtualizes simple straight-line code (and code with `if/else` joins where merges happen but loops do not).

**Deliverables**:
- Tier-1 handling: detect `jeandle.new_instance` and `jeandle.newarray` invokes, create VirtualObject + ObjectState, push alias.
- Tier-2 handling: GEP+store and GEP+load on virtual base — update ObjectState, record effects.
- Tier-3 simplification (limited): identity comparisons against `null`.
- Effect recording: `RecordAliasMapping`, `ReplaceUsesWith`, `EraseInstruction`.
- Basic call escape: any `call`/`invoke` with a virtual operand that doesn't match a recognized virtualizable pattern triggers materialization.

**Tests**: lit tests `01_alloc_unused.ll`, `02_alloc_store_load.ll`, `03_alloc_escapes_call.ll`, `04_alloc_escapes_return.ll`, `05_null_check_folded.ll`.

**Files modified**: `PartialEscapeAnalysis.cpp`, `PartialEscapeTransform.cpp`.

**Dependencies**: Phase 2.

### Phase 4: Merges and PHI (2 weeks)

**Goal**: correct virtualization across `if/else`/`switch` merges, including PHI virtualization and incompatible-merge materialization.

**Deliverables**:
- `PEABlockState::merge(predStates...)`: state union with iterative stabilization (`do { … } while (materialized)`).
- PHI handling at join blocks: detect compatible vs incompatible virtual merges, emit `CreatePHI` + `SetPHIIncoming` effects.
- `select i1 %c, %a, %b` handled identically to PHI.
- PHI in alias map (alias of PHI = merged virtual id, or materialized ptr).

**Tests**: `06_phi_same_object.ll`, `07_phi_compatible_merge.ll`, `08_phi_incompatible_merge.ll`, `09_select_virtual.ll`, `10_phi_virtual_and_materialized.ll`, `11_merge_field_state.ll`.

**Dependencies**: Phase 3.

### Phase 5: Invoke and Exception Edges (1 week)

**Goal**: correct state splitting at `InvokeInst`s; virtual objects in `"deopt"` bundles are treated as escapes (since Jeandle has no deopt support, but the bundle's presence indicates the value is consumed by VM machinery).

**Deliverables**:
- Two-successor state propagation at `InvokeInst`: normal-dest gets the post-call state; unwind-dest gets the pre-call state (state is restored because the exceptional control flow happens before side effects).
- Materialization of virtual arguments before non-virtualizable invokes (already implemented in Phase 3, but exception edges expose corner cases).
- Tracking of the `"deopt"` bundle: any virtual referenced by the bundle is materialized (since Jeandle's PEA does not emit `VirtualObjectState`).

**Tests**: `12_invoke_exception_state.ll`, `13_invoke_with_virtual_arg.ll`, `14_invoke_deopt_bundle.ll`.

**Dependencies**: Phase 4.

### Phase 6: Loops (3 weeks)

**Goal**: correct, terminating analysis of loops, with effect-list discarding on non-convergence.

**Deliverables**:
- Per-block effect snapshot/restore (`EffectList::truncateTo(size_t)`), mirroring Graal's ability to drop effects added during a discarded iteration.
- Fixpoint loop iteration with `MAX_ITERATIONS = 10` (matching Graal's default) and a `MATERIALIZE_ALL` fallback mode on overflow.
- `EffectsClosureMode::REGULAR_VIRTUALIZATION` vs `STOP_NEW_VIRTUALIZATIONS` vs `MATERIALIZE_ALL` modes.
- `LoopCutoffDepth = 4` for deep nests (Graal's default).
- Loop-exit proxy PHI emission (in the loop-exit block).
- Field instability widening: if a tracked field changes value on every iteration, widen it to "must materialize."

**Tests**: `20_loop_object_outside.ll`, `21_loop_alloc_inside.ll`, `22_loop_field_updated.ll`, `23_nested_loop.ll`, `24_loop_convergence_failure.ll`, `25_loop_exit_proxy_phi.ll`, `26_loop_cutoff_depth.ll`.

**Dependencies**: Phase 4 (merges).

### Phase 7: Advanced Features (2-3 weeks)

**Goal**: lock elision, type-check folding, array virtualization, nested virtual objects, identity comparison folding.

**Deliverables**:
- Lock elision: `LockCount` tracking on ObjectState, eliminated-lock recording, lock restoration list for materialization.
- Type-check folding: `jeandle.checkcast`/`check_instanceof`/`instanceof` on virtual objects, via `VMCallbacks::IsSubtype`.
- `jeandle.arraylength` and `jeandle.load_klass` folding to constants.
- Array virtualization: `JeandleArrayVirtualizationLimit = 32` (matches Graal's `MaximumEscapeAnalysisArrayLength`); constant-index access required.
- Nested virtual objects: VirtualRef field values, recursive materialization (cycle detection forces materialization on cycles).
- Identity comparison folding: `icmp eq/ne` on virtual / virtual+null / virtual+non-virtual.
- `jeandle.check_if_value_based` handling: VMCallbacks query.

**Tests**: `30_lock_elision.ll`, `31_nested_locks.ll`, `32_reentrant_locks.ll`, `33_lock_partial_escape.ll`, `34_checkcast_folded.ll`, `35_instanceof_folded.ll`, `36_arraylength_folded.ll`, `37_array_const_index.ll`, `38_array_var_index_materialize.ll`, `39_nested_virtual.ll`, `40_cyclic_virtual.ll`, `41_identity_eq.ll`.

**Dependencies**: Phase 6.

### Phase 8: Transform Pass (2 weeks)

**Goal**: correct, SSA-preserving, dominance-preserving IR rewrite.

**Deliverables**:
- Two-pass effect application (non-CFG-kill, then CFG-kill).
- Materialization code generator: block split, invoke insertion, field stores, lock calls, deopt-bundle propagation, calling-convention preservation, DebugLoc propagation, attribute and metadata propagation.
- PHI creation with predecessor-keyed input wiring.
- Allocation elimination with poison RAUW + branch rewrite.
- Lock elimination with i1-result RAUW and branch simplification.
- Dead-code cleanup (unreachable blocks, SimplifyCFG, simplifyInstruction, trivial-PHI removal).
- Debug verifier (Section 3.8) gated on `#ifndef NDEBUG`.

**Tests**: `50_transform_simple.ll`, `51_transform_blocksplit.ll`, `52_transform_grouped_mat.ll`, `53_transform_phi_input_order.ll`, `54_transform_loop_exit.ll`, `55_transform_dominance.ll`, `56_transform_metadata.ll`, `57_transform_deopt_bundle.ll`.

**Dependencies**: Phase 7.

### Phase 9: Integration and Testing (2 weeks)

**Goal**: end-to-end correctness with Jeandle's pipeline; jtreg green; no perf regressions.

**Deliverables**:
- Pipeline integration: replace the Phase 1 `RequireAnalysisPass` no-op with the real analysis + transform; verify pass ordering w.r.t. `JavaOperationLower(0)`.
- jtreg test suite: `jeandle-jdk/test/hotspot/jtreg/compiler/jeandle/partialEscape/` with Java-source-level tests (see Section 8.4).
- Run the existing `compiler/jeandle/` jtreg tests with `-XX:+UseJeandleCompiler -XX:+JeandleDumpIR` to verify no behavioural regressions.
- Add `-XX:+PrintPEAStatistics` flag for diagnostic output (counts virtualized, materialized, locks elided per method).

**Tests**: `partialEscape/TestBasicElimination.java`, `TestSyncEliminationOnVirtual.java`, `TestArrayVirtualization.java`, `TestEscapeOnReturn.java`, `TestEscapeOnException.java`, `TestNestedVirtual.java`, `TestPhiMerge.java`, `TestLoopVirtualization.java`.

**Dependencies**: Phase 8.

### Phase 10: Hardening (ongoing)

**Goal**: edge-case robustness, crash-free on adversarial input, useful debug output.

**Deliverables**:
- Edge cases (Phase 7 §4.7): atomicrmw / cmpxchg / memcpy / addrspacecast / ptrtoint on virtual operands.
- `JeandleCrashOnError` integration: if PEA hits an unhandled IR pattern in debug builds, fail loudly; in release builds, conservatively bail out (return `PreservedAnalyses::all()` without modifying IR).
- Debug output: `-debug-only=partial-escape-analysis` and `-debug-only=partial-escape-transform`; per-method statistics dump under `-XX:+PrintPEAStatistics`.
- Memory: per-function `BumpPtrAllocator` for ObjectState / EffectList to avoid `malloc` churn on large methods.
- Fuzz testing: random LLVM IR generator constrained to Jeandle's IR shapes.

**Dependencies**: Phase 9.

---

## 8. Test Scenarios

Tests are organized into four tiers: (1) LLVM lit FileCheck tests at the IR level; (2) gtest unit tests for data structures; (3) jtreg end-to-end Java tests; (4) JMH micro-benchmarks for performance validation. Tests are stored under `llvm/test/Jeandle/partial-escape/`, `llvm/unittests/Jeandle/`, `jeandle-jdk/test/hotspot/jtreg/compiler/jeandle/partialEscape/`, and `jeandle-jdk/test/micro/org/openjdk/bench/jeandle/pea/` respectively.

Every lit test follows the established pattern (see `llvm/test/Jeandle/type-check-elimination/instanceof-known-subtype.ll`):

```
; RUN: opt -S -passes="require<partial-escape-analysis>,partial-escape-transform" %s | FileCheck %s
declare ... @jeandle.new_instance(...)
...
define ... @test() gc "hotspotgc" { ... }
; CHECK-NOT: jeandle.new_instance
!java-method-compilation = !{}
```

### 8.1 lit FileCheck tests (LLVM IR level)

#### 8.1.1 Allocations

| Test | Description | Input IR Sketch | Expected Output | CHECK directives |
|------|-------------|-----------------|-----------------|-------------------|
| `01_alloc_unused.ll` | Allocation with no uses is removed entirely. | `invoke @jeandle.new_instance(klass, 16) … ; ret void` | The invoke and its unwind block are gone; `ret void` remains. | `CHECK-NOT: jeandle.new_instance` + `CHECK: ret void` |
| `02_alloc_store_load.ll` | Object allocated, one int field stored and read, never escapes. | `%o = invoke @jeandle.new_instance(...)` then `store i32 42, %o.f`; `%v = load i32, %o.f`; `ret i32 %v`. | All ops gone; `ret i32 42`. | `CHECK-NOT: jeandle.new_instance` / `CHECK: ret i32 42` |
| `03_alloc_full_escape.ll` | Object passed to external call → no optimization. | `%o = invoke @jeandle.new_instance(...)`; `call void @extern(%o)`. | Allocation preserved unchanged. | `CHECK: jeandle.new_instance` |
| `04_alloc_escapes_one_branch.ll` | Allocation escapes only on the `then` branch; the `else` branch returns a constant. | `if (cond) { extern(%o); } else { ret 0 }` | Materialization sunk into `then`; `else` has no allocation. | `CHECK: then:` followed by `CHECK: jeandle.new_instance`; `CHECK: else:` followed by `CHECK-NOT: jeandle.new_instance` |
| `05_alloc_escapes_return.ll` | Returns a freshly allocated object. | `%o = invoke @jeandle.new_instance(...); ret %o` | Allocation preserved (escapes via return). | `CHECK: jeandle.new_instance` / `CHECK: ret ptr addrspace(1)` |
| `06_alloc_array.ll` | `jeandle.newarray` with constant length 4. | `invoke @jeandle.newarray(arrayKlass, 4)` then index 0..3 stores. | Allocation eliminated; loads folded. | `CHECK-NOT: jeandle.newarray` |
| `07_alloc_array_large.ll` | `jeandle.newarray` length 64 (> limit). | Same shape with length 64. | Allocation NOT eliminated. | `CHECK: jeandle.newarray` |
| `08_alloc_array_var_length.ll` | `jeandle.newarray` with non-constant length. | length is a function arg. | Allocation NOT eliminated. | `CHECK: jeandle.newarray` |
| `09_alloc_in_unreachable_block.ll` | Allocation in an unreachable block (after `unreachable` inst). | block reachable only after `unreachable`. | Allocation removed with the unreachable block. | `CHECK-NOT: jeandle.new_instance` |

#### 8.1.2 Field accesses (store / load)

| Test | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `10_field_overwrite.ll` | `obj.f = 5; obj.f = 10` → only `10` flows. |
| `11_field_load_after_store.ll` | RAW dependency folded; load returns stored value. |
| `12_field_partial_escape.ll` | Store + load on hot path; escape on cold path → materialized on cold path with current field values. |
| `13_field_unwritten_default_zero.ll` | Load from an un-stored field → folded to 0 / null. |
| `14_field_indirect_via_phi.ll` | Field value flows through PHI in a downstream block. |
| `15_field_metadata_preserved.ll` | Original load carries `!nonnull`; replacement value derives `!nonnull` correctly. |

#### 8.1.3 Locks (monitorenter / monitorexit)

| Test | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `16_lock_simple_elision.ll` | `synchronized(new Object())` → enter/exit gone; slow path block removed. |
| `17_lock_nested_on_two_virtuals.ll` | Two non-escaping objects locked in nested order → all locks removed. |
| `18_lock_reentrant.ll` | Same virtual object locked twice → LockCount=2 then 0; both pairs removed. |
| `19_lock_escapes_on_branch.ll` | Object escapes on one branch → lock re-emitted at materialization point on escaping branch. |
| `20_lock_unbalanced.ll` | LockCount != 0 at exit → force materialization, locks restored. |
| `21_lock_value_based.ll` | `jeandle.check_if_value_based` returns true on a virtual → object materialized (cannot virtualize). |

#### 8.1.4 Array virtualization

| Test | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `22_array_constant_index_access.ll` | `arr[0] = x; arr[2] = y; load arr[2]` → folded. |
| `23_array_var_index_materialize.ll` | `arr[i]` with non-constant i → materialization. |
| `24_arraylength_folded.ll` | `jeandle.arraylength(virtArr)` → constant. |
| `25_array_store_check_no_escape.ll` | `jeandle.array_store_check(val, virtArr)` → does not cause array to escape (val may). |

#### 8.1.5 Type checks (checkcast, instanceof, load_klass)

| Test | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `26_checkcast_known_exact.ll` | `checkcast(SuperK, virtObj)` where exact klass is subtype → fold to true; branch simplified. |
| `27_instanceof_known_exact.ll` | `instanceof` returns i32 0/1. |
| `28_check_instanceof_returns_i1.ll` | `check_instanceof` i1 fold. |
| `29_load_klass_folded.ll` | `load_klass(virt)` → `inttoptr (i64 KlassEnc to ptr)`. |
| `30_checkcast_incompatible.ll` | `checkcast` to incompatible klass → false; dead branch eliminated. |

#### 8.1.6 Identity / null comparisons

| Test | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `31_null_check_on_virtual.ll` | `icmp eq virt, null` → `false`; branch simplified. |
| `32_eq_same_virtual.ll` | `icmp eq %a, %a` where both alias to V_5 → `true`. |
| `33_eq_different_virtual.ll` | Different IDs → `false`. |
| `34_eq_virtual_vs_external.ll` | One side virtual, other external → `false`. |

#### 8.1.7 PHI / merge

| Test | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `35_phi_same_object.ll` | Same V_k from both branches → alias rewrites; no PHI created. |
| `36_phi_compatible_merge.ll` | Two distinct virtuals of same type, both single-use → merged into one V_m with per-field PHIs. |
| `37_phi_incompatible_types.ll` | Different types → both materialized in predecessor blocks; standard PHI of ptrs. |
| `38_phi_identity_observable.ll` | One of the virtuals has `==` comparison elsewhere → identity observable → materialized. |
| `39_phi_virtual_and_materialized.ll` | One incoming is virtual, other is already materialized → virtual one materialized in its predecessor. |
| `40_phi_field_state_merge.ll` | Same V_k from both branches, but field `f` differs → field becomes PHI; alias unchanged. |

#### 8.1.8 Select

| Test | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `41_select_virtual_virtual.ll` | Same logic as PHI but with `select`. |
| `42_select_virtual_constant.ll` | `select c, %virt, null` → virtualize cleanly if alternative is null; otherwise materialize. |

#### 8.1.9 Loops

| Test | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `43_loop_object_outside.ll` | Object alive before loop, not modified inside → stays virtual across loop. |
| `44_loop_field_updated.ll` | Field updated in each iteration → loop-exit PHI for field value, no allocation. |
| `45_loop_alloc_inside_dead.ll` | Allocation inside loop with no escape and no live-out → eliminated. |
| `46_loop_alloc_inside_live_out.ll` | Allocation inside loop, last iteration value escapes after loop → materialized at loop exit. |
| `47_loop_nested.ll` | Inner-loop virtual referenced by outer-loop virtual; both eliminate. |
| `48_loop_conditional_escape.ll` | Object escapes only on one inner branch → materialization on that branch. |
| `49_loop_deep_nest.ll` | Nesting depth > LoopCutoffDepth → STOP_NEW_VIRTUALIZATIONS mode. |

#### 8.1.10 Loop convergence failure / MATERIALIZE_ALL

| Test | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `50_loop_field_changes_every_iter.ll` | Field instability heuristic triggers materialization. |
| `51_loop_max_iterations.ll` | Loop hits MAX_ITERATIONS=10 → MATERIALIZE_ALL fallback; verify pass terminates and IR is valid. |
| `52_loop_overflow_rollback.ll` | OverflowException propagation; state rollback to pre-loop. |

#### 8.1.11 Loop exit PHI / proxy

| Test | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `53_loop_exit_proxy_phi.ll` | Loop body materializes; loop exit gets PHI joining body and preheader (preheader value = poison if not live). |
| `54_lcssa_compatibility.ll` | Verify PEA-emitted PHIs are LCSSA-friendly when the function is in LCSSA. |

#### 8.1.12 Materialization placement

| Test | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `55_materialize_block_split.ll` | Escape mid-block → block split correctly, materialization invoke becomes the new terminator. |
| `56_materialize_grouped.ll` | Two virtual objects (A references B) materialize at same point → B emitted before A. |
| `57_materialize_unwind_reuse.ll` | Materialization reuses the enclosing invoke's unwind destination. |
| `58_materialize_unwind_synthesize.ll` | No enclosing handler exists → synthetic landingpad+resume block created. |
| `59_materialize_preserves_deopt_bundle.ll` | Materialization invoke carries `"deopt"()` bundle matching the original. |
| `60_materialize_preserves_callingconv.ll` | Materialization invoke uses `hotspotcc` (CallingConv::Hotspot_JIT). |
| `61_materialize_preserves_klass_attrs.ll` | Materialization invoke carries `java-klass=N` and `java-klass-exact` return attrs. |
| `62_materialize_preserves_dbg.ll` | Materialization invoke carries `!dbg` from the original allocation. |

#### 8.1.13 Recursive / nested virtual objects

| Test | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `63_nested_virtual_eliminated.ll` | A.f = B; both never escape → both removed. |
| `64_nested_virtual_outer_escapes.ll` | A escapes; B is referenced only by A.f → both materialize at A's escape point, B first. |
| `65_cyclic_virtual_force_materialize.ll` | A.f = B; B.f = A → cycle detected, both materialized at first use. |

#### 8.1.14 Exception edge state splitting

| Test | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `66_invoke_exception_state_split.ll` | After `invoke`, virtual object exists only on normal-dest path. |
| `67_invoke_unwind_uses_virtual.ll` | Unwind dest reads from a field that was only written post-invoke → fold uses zero (pre-invoke state). |
| `68_invoke_virtual_arg.ll` | Calling a non-virtualizable invoke with a virtual arg → materialize before the invoke; materialization invoke uses same unwind dest. |

#### 8.1.15 Atomic operations and odd LLVM constructs

| Test | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `69_atomicrmw_on_virtual_field.ll` | Forces materialization. |
| `70_cmpxchg_on_virtual_field.ll` | Forces materialization. |
| `71_memcpy_to_virtual.ll` | Const-length memcpy whose span equals the virtual layout → field-set; non-const → materialize. |
| `72_memset_virtual_zeroes.ll` | `memset 0` over virtual layout → all fields set to zero (no-op since they default to zero). |
| `73_ptrtoint_virtual.ll` | Forces materialization (identity-as-integer escapes). |
| `74_addrspacecast_virtual.ll` | Forces materialization. |
| `75_freeze_virtual.ll` | Alias-preserving; freeze removed during cleanup. |
| `76_llvm_assume_on_virt.ll` | `assume` referencing eliminated load is rewritten or dropped. |

#### 8.1.16 Field instability heuristic

| Test | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `77_field_instability_widens.ll` | Field tracked through a 3-iteration loop converges; another field with different value each iter widens. |

#### 8.1.17 Pipeline integration

| Test | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `78_pipeline_pea_to_lower.ll` | Run `pea-analysis,pea-transform,java-operation-lower<phase=0>` and verify the materialized allocations get inlined only by phase-1 lowering when the full pipeline runs. |
| `79_pipeline_no_barrier_on_eliminated_store.ll` | After PEA + InsertGCBarriers, eliminated stores have no barrier. |
| `80_pipeline_pea_with_rs4gc.ll` | End-to-end including `RewriteStatepointsForGC` → materialized allocations are statepointed correctly. |

### 8.2 gtest unit tests (`llvm/unittests/Jeandle/PartialEscapeTest.cpp`)

- `FieldValueRoundtripTest` — constructing each variant and reading it back.
- `ObjectStateCloneTest` — clone is deep, mutation of clone doesn't affect original.
- `MergeIdenticalStatesTest` — merge(S, S) == S; no effects emitted.
- `MergeDifferingFieldTest` — merge emits one `CreatePHI` per differing field.
- `MergeIncompatibleTest` — incompatible types force materialization on both sides.
- `AliasMapBasicTest` — set/get/clear; HasVirtualInputs propagation.
- `EffectListOrderTest` — `add` preserves insertion order; `truncateTo(K)` correctly discards effects with seq >= K.
- `EffectListTruncateMidApplyTest` — apply pass aborts cleanly when an effect throws.
- `LockCountTrackingTest` — nested/reentrant enter/exit balance correctly.
- `LoopCutoffDepthTest` — `STOP_NEW_VIRTUALIZATIONS` mode set at depth 5.
- `MaxIterationsFallbackTest` — convergence failure triggers `MATERIALIZE_ALL`.

### 8.3 Cross-referencing Graal's test suite

Graal's PEA test classes (in `/home/taofengliu/graal/compiler/src/jdk.graal.compiler.test/src/jdk/graal/compiler/core/test/ea/`) and corresponding Jeandle coverage:

| Graal test class | Concept tested | Jeandle counterpart |
|------------------|----------------|---------------------|
| `EscapeAnalysisTest.java` | Basic escape analysis cases. | lit `01_*` through `09_*` |
| `PartialEscapeAnalysisTest.java` | Partial escape (sinking allocations into escaping branches). | lit `04_*`, `19_*`, `48_*`; jtreg `TestBasicElimination` |
| `EAMergingTest.java` | PHI merges. | lit `35_*` through `40_*`; jtreg `TestPhiMerge` |
| `PartialEscapeAnalysisIterationTest.java` | Iterative merge stabilization. | lit `51_*`, `52_*`; jtreg `TestLoopVirtualization` |
| `PartialEscapeAnalysisTreesTest.java` | Nested virtual objects (trees). | lit `63_*`, `64_*`, `65_*`; jtreg `TestNestedVirtual` |
| `NestedBoxingTest.java` | Boxing-heavy loop elimination. | jtreg `TestBoxingInLoop`; JMH `BoxingSumBenchmark` |
| `PartialEscapeWithExceptionTest.java` | Allocation on a path that throws. | lit `66_*`, `67_*`, `68_*`; jtreg `TestEscapeOnException` |
| `AtomicVirtualizationTests.java` | atomicrmw/cmpxchg behavior. | lit `69_*`, `70_*` |
| `UnsafeCompareAndSwapVirtualizationTest.java` | sun.misc.Unsafe CAS. | (out of scope for Jeandle initial release; document as future work) |
| `UnsafeEATest.java` | Unsafe accessor patterns. | (out of scope initially) |
| `PEAAssertionsTest.java` | Java assertions don't break virtualization. | jtreg `TestAssertionsCompatibility` |
| `PoorMansEATest.java` | Trivial EA cases that should always succeed. | lit `01_*`, `02_*` |
| `CountUppercaseParallelTest.java` | Stream-heavy workload. | jtreg `TestStreamWorkload` |
| `PartialEscapeOptimizationLogTest.java` | Optimization log output. | Jeandle `-XX:+PrintPEAStatistics` smoke test |

| `ExpandLogicTest.java` | Logic-expansion interaction. | not directly applicable (Jeandle uses separate `TypeCheckElimination`) |
| `TrufflePEATest.java` | Truffle-specific patterns. | not applicable |
| `ReadEliminationTest.java`, `FinalReadEliminationTest.java`, `PEAReadEliminationTest.java`, `ReadEliminationCodeEmissionTest.java` | Read elimination (separate phase in Graal). | explicitly deferred per Design Decision 5.11 |

### 8.4 jtreg tests (`jeandle-jdk/test/hotspot/jtreg/compiler/jeandle/partialEscape/`)

Each test is structured like the existing `compiler/jeandle/typeCheckElimination/*.java` tests: a small Java program annotated with `@test`, run under `-XX:+UseJeandleCompiler -XX:+JeandleDumpIR -XX:JeandleDumpDirectory=...` plus assertions on the generated IR via `FileCheck` (or behavioural assertions on output).

| Test class | Scenario | Expected behaviour |
|------------|----------|--------------------|
| `TestBasicElimination.java` | `class Box { int v; } int f() { Box b = new Box(); b.v = 7; return b.v; }` | IR after PEA contains no `jeandle.new_instance`; method returns 7. |
| `TestSyncEliminationOnVirtual.java` | `int f() { Object o = new Object(); synchronized(o) { return 1; } }` | No `monitorenter`/`monitorexit`/`new_instance`. |
| `TestArrayVirtualization.java` | `int f() { int[] a = new int[4]; a[0]=1; a[1]=2; a[2]=3; a[3]=4; return a[2]; }` | No `newarray`; returns 3. |
| `TestArrayLengthFold.java` | `int f() { return new int[7].length; }` | Returns 7; no `newarray`, no `arraylength`. |
| `TestEscapeOnReturn.java` | `Object f() { return new Object(); }` | Allocation preserved (returns escape). |
| `TestEscapeOnException.java` | `void f() throws X { Object o = new Object(); if (cond) throw new X(); g(o); }` | Allocation of `Object` moved to the non-throw branch; allocation of `X` preserved. |
| `TestNestedVirtual.java` | `class Outer { Inner i; } class Inner { int x; } int f() { Outer o = new Outer(); o.i = new Inner(); o.i.x = 5; return o.i.x; }` | Both `new_instance`s removed; returns 5. |
| `TestPhiMerge.java` | `int f(boolean c) { Box b = c ? new Box(1) : new Box(2); return b.v; }` | Both allocations removed; `b.v` is `phi i32 [1, ...], [2, ...]`. |
| `TestLoopVirtualization.java` | `int f(int n) { Box b = new Box(0); for (int i=0; i<n; i++) b.v += i; return b.v; }` | No allocation; `b.v` becomes a loop PHI summing 0..n-1. |
| `TestLoopAllocInside.java` | `int f(int n) { int s=0; for(int i=0;i<n;i++){ Box b=new Box(i); s+=b.v; } return s; }` | No allocations; `s` accumulates `i`. |
| `TestSyncOnEscapingObject.java` | `void f(Object o) { synchronized(o) { ... } }` | Lock NOT eliminated (object is a parameter). |
| `TestCheckcastFoldedOnNew.java` | `int f() { Object o = new Integer(5); return ((Integer)o).intValue(); }` | `checkcast` folded; allocation may or may not survive depending on `intValue` virtualizability. |
| `TestIdentityComparisonOnNew.java` | `boolean f() { Object a = new Object(); Object b = new Object(); return a == b; }` | Returns false; both allocations gone. |
| `TestValueBasedClass.java` | `void f() { synchronized(Optional.empty()) { ... } }` | `Optional` is value-based → object materialized; verify no `IllegalMonitorStateException`-causing bug. |
| `TestRegressionExistingMonitor.java` | Re-run existing `compiler/jeandle/Monitor/*` tests with PEA enabled. | All existing assertions hold. |

### 8.5 Benchmarking (`jeandle-jdk/test/micro/org/openjdk/bench/jeandle/pea/`)

JMH benchmarks to confirm PEA delivers the expected runtime wins. All benchmarks compare `-XX:+UseJeandleCompiler -XX:-PartialEscapeAnalysis` (baseline) against `-XX:+PartialEscapeAnalysis` (treatment), and optionally against HotSpot's C2 for sanity.

| Benchmark | Scenario | Expected speedup |
|-----------|----------|------------------|
| `BoxingSumBenchmark` | Sum integers via autoboxing: `Integer sum=0; for(int i=0;i<N;i++) sum+=i;`. | 2–5× — boxing allocations entirely eliminated. |
| `OptionalChainBenchmark` | Chained `Optional.of(...).map(...).orElse(...)`. | 1.5–3× — `Optional` allocations eliminated. |
| `IteratorAllocBenchmark` | `for (X x : collection) ...` where iterator allocates per call. | 1.5–2× — iterator allocation eliminated. |
| `LocalEscapingPairBenchmark` | Pair-like helper object passed to inlined hot method. | 1.5–2× — pair object scalarized. |
| `LockOnLocalBenchmark` | Synchronized block on locally-allocated object (common in legacy `Vector`/`StringBuffer` patterns). | 3–10× — both allocation and lock acquisition eliminated. |
| `EscapeOnExceptionBenchmark` | Object escapes only on exceptional path (rare). | ~equal or slightly faster — allocation sunk into cold branch. |
| `MicroPhiMergeBenchmark` | `Box b = cond ? new Box(1) : new Box(2); use(b.v);`. | 2–4× — both allocations eliminated. |
| `LoopAccumulatorBenchmark` | Loop-local accumulator object updated each iteration. | 3–6×. |
| `NestedEscapeBenchmark` | Nested Outer/Inner allocations, neither escapes. | 2–3×. |
| `RegressionMacroBenchmark` | A non-PEA-friendly workload (escaping allocations dominant) to ensure PEA does not regress. | Within 2% of baseline. |

Benchmark harness is JMH (already used in `jeandle-jdk/test/micro/org/openjdk/bench/jeandle/`). Each benchmark records:

1. `Mode.AverageTime` in `Mode.NANOSECONDS`.
2. `gc.alloc.rate.norm` (per-op allocation rate) — for PEA-friendly benchmarks this should drop to near-zero.
3. `-prof perfasm` (Linux) to spot-check that the JIT output for hot loops contains no allocation slow-paths.

A regression test in CI runs `RegressionMacroBenchmark` and `BoxingSumBenchmark` on every PR touching `PartialEscape*.cpp` and gates on a 5% tolerance band.
